# THE GOLDEN AGE REBORN
<note>original: "AVREVM SECVLVM REDIVIVVM"</note>
->That is<-
## The ancient, vanished Golden Age,
->Which has now risen again, blossomed lovably, and set fragrant Golden Seeds.<-
->Which precious and noble seed is shown and revealed to all true sons of wisdom and learning:<-
<note>original: "sapientiæ & doctrinæ filiis"</note>
### HENRICUS MADATHANUS, THEOSOPHER,
<note>Henricus Madathanus is a pseudonym for the German physician and alchemist Adrian von Mynsicht (1603–1638).</note>
->Physician and finally, by the grace of God, a brother of the Golden Cross.<-
<note>original: "Medicus & tandem, Dei gratia aureæ crucis frater"</note>

<image-desc>A complex alchemical circular emblem. The outer ring contains the Latin inscription: "THREE ARE WONDERFUL: GOD AND MAN, MOTHER AND VIRGIN, THREE AND ONE" <note>original: "TRIA SUNT MIRABILIA, DEUS ET HOMO, MATER ET VIRGO, TRINUS ET UNUS"</note>. Inside this is a square, and within the square is a triangle pointing upwards. The triangle contains the text "THE CENTER IN THE TRIANGLE" <note>original: "CENTRUM IN TRIGONO"</note> along its sides. At the very center of the triangle is a point within a small circle. To the left and right of the triangle, within the square, are the letters "B" and "S". A small cross sits atop the outermost circle of the emblem.</image-desc>

<margin>James in Epistle chapter 1, verse 5.</margin>

> If any of you lacks wisdom, let him ask of God, who gives generously to all without reproach, and it will be given to him.

->THE AUTHOR’S SYMBOL.<-
->Center of the world, grain of the deep.<-
<note>original: "Centrum mundi, granum fundi." This refers to the idea that the most potent power is hidden in the smallest, most central point of matter.</note>

# Preface
->To the Christian and worthy Reader.<-

Friendly, God-loving Reader, and especially you sons of wisdom and learning <note>original: "sapientiæ & doctrinæ filij"</note>, some years ago, Almighty God—at my constant, fervent prayer and diligent calling—opened my eyes through the enlightenment of His Holy Spirit (from whom we receive all wisdom, and who was sent to us from the Father through Christ). He opened them so far that I recognized the true center in the triangle of the center <note>original: "Centrum in Trigono centri"</note>, the single and true material of the noble Philosopher’s Stone, and essentially held it in my hands. Yet, for nearly five years, I did not know how to proceed with it profitably, rightly, and properly; how to separate the blood of the Red Lion and the gluten of the White Eagle <note>The "Red Lion" and "White Eagle" are alchemical metaphors for fixed and volatile substances, often associated with gold and mercury or acids.</note> from it, much less how to mix, enclose, seal, and commit them to the secret fire according to the proportioned weight of Nature. This must be done with great reason and careful caution. 

And although I searched with special and notable diligence through the writings, parables, and various figures of the Philosophers, and took great pains to solve their manifold, miraculous riddles <note>original: "ænigmata"</note>—some of which grew out of their own brains—I found in reality <note>original: "reipsa"</note> that it is all together pure fantasy and foolishness. The *Aurora of Philosophers* likewise testifies to this, namely: that all the preparations described by Geber, Albertus Magnus <note>Famous medieval alchemists whose works were often interpreted too literally by later "sophists."</note>, and their kind, including their purifications, sublimations, cementations, distillations, rectifications, circulations, putrefactions, conjunctions, solutions, ascensions, coagulations, calcinations, incinerations, mortifications, reunifications, etc.—as well as their tripods, Athanors <note>A slow-burning furnace used by alchemists.</note>, reverberatory furnaces, melting furnaces, rotting-tubs, horse dung, ash, sand, cucurbits <note>A gourd-shaped laboratory vessel.</note>, pelicans <note>A circulatory distillation vessel.</note>, vials, retorts, fixing-vessels, etc.—are all pure sophistry, deceptive and useless things. I must, for my part, confess this in truth. 

For noble Nature, which gladly allows itself to be found in its own innate substance, knows nothing of these things. Therefore, Theophrastus <note>Paracelsus (Philippus Aureolus Theophrastus Bombastus von Hohenheim), the famous Swiss physician and alchemist.</note> in *The Magical Secret of the Philosopher's Stone* <note>original: "Secreto Magico de Lap. Philos."</note> says rightly of those who seek the material of the stone in wine, in imperfect bodies, in blood, in marcasite, in mercury, in sulfur, in urine, in dung, in arsenic <note>original: "auripigmento"</note>, and in herbs such as celandine, spring-root, ivy, hyssop, etc.: It is all together roguery and thievery with which they mislead people, costing them their money, consuming and losing their time uselessly and in vain. They follow only their own foolish heads, which cannot calculate beforehand what Nature requires. 

Dearly, tell me this: who must burn water for me in the minerals of the earth? Or are there people inside who distill wine or urinate child’s urine to make metals? Or do you think there are apothecaries inside who have everything for sale so that you can make metals? You fool, can you not understand that you err, as none of those things belong to Nature? Or do you wish to be above God, that you want to make metal out of blood? Then make a human out of a horse, or a cow out of a mouse, which might then give you good milk—that would also be a multiplication! But it does not happen. And just as little as that can happen, so little can you make metals from the aforementioned recipes, for the art is not outside of Nature; and what Nature has 

<column-break/>

created, there art can have no effect. When a woman bears a little boy, art cannot make a little girl out of it, no matter what thing is used for that purpose. 

From this, anyone can easily judge how, in what manner, and in what the blessed material <note>original: "materia benedicta"</note> should be sought and found. But let no one imagine, much less be persuaded by any "artists," that even if he truly knows, recognizes, and holds the true material in his hands—either through the secret revelation of God or from those who know it—that he then equally understands how to unlock it, or how to separate and purify the pure from the impure to the highest degree. Oh no, dear laborer, not by a long shot! "The dog lies buried in the bite" <note>A German idiom meaning "that is where the real difficulty lies."</note> right from the start. Art and a skillful mind are required for the matter. Look, for example, at what you heard from me at the beginning: that I knew the true material of the stone for five years, and the whole time I did not know how to proceed with it, until finally, after the sixth year, the key of power was entrusted to me through secret revelation from Almighty God. 

And the ancient Patriarchs, Prophets, and Philosophers always kept this key secret and hidden. For the Monarch <note>Referring to Paracelsus.</note> says in the cited place: "Had they written it openly so that every shoemaker or tooth-puller could understand it, that would be a great theft; it would no longer be a secret, and much evil could be committed through it, which would then be acting openly against the Will of God," etc. 

For these reasons, and those I have partly mentioned in the Epilogue, and so that I would not be seen as burying the talent committed to me by God <note>original: "talentum à Deo mihi commissum"</note>, I have wished to reveal in this, my *Golden Age Reborn*, as much as God and Nature allow, the great secret of the Wise. My eyes have seen it, my hands have prepared it, and through the grace of God, I have taken it up again in due time in great power and glory. May the pious and God-loving reader recognize, accept, and receive it in no other way than for the good; consider it rightly <note>original: "dextre"</note>, and not be misled or troubled by the fact that, at times, contradictions appear according to the letter. It could not have been arranged otherwise, nor would it have been fitting, to step from theory to practice <note>original: "per Theoriam ad praxin"</note>. To write more clearly and plainly about this is earnestly and most highly forbidden in the Chemical Republic <note>original: "republica chymica"</note>. 

However, I have no doubt that all those who look upon this little treatise in true confidence with the inner eyes of the mind—which can accomplish all things—who study it diligently, and above all, call upon God inwardly and from the heart, will, like me, enjoy the hidden Philosophical wonder-fruits and become partakers of them according to the will of God. And then they are and remain true brothers of the Golden Cross and chosen members of the Philosophical community in eternal alliance.

Finally, so that the Christian, sensible, and worthy reader may actually know my Christian name and surname, I will be so candid <note>original: "candidus"</note> as to reveal it in the following way, so that no one can rightfully complain about me: Know then, everyone, that the number of my name is M. DCXII. <note>1612</note>. In this number, my entire name has been perfectly written into the Book of Nature through 11 dead and 7 living [letters]. Moreover, the 5th letter is the fifth part of the 8th, and the 15th is again the fifth part of the 12th. Let this suffice for you.

->Given at the Fir Mountain, the 25th of March in the Year 1621.<-
<note>original: "Datum in Monte abiegno". "Monte Abiegno" or "Mount Fir" is a recurring symbolic location in Rosicrucian literature.</note>

<summary>This title page and preface introduce the "Golden Age Reborn," where the author (using a pseudonym) claims to reveal the true secret of the Philosopher's Stone through divine revelation after years of failed experimentation with common alchemical methods. He emphasizes that the "art" must follow Nature and warns against the false "sophistical" recipes of medieval alchemy.</summary>
<keywords>Henricus Madathanus, Adrian von Mynsicht, alchemy, Philosopher's Stone, Rosicrucianism, Golden Cross, Red Lion, White Eagle, Paracelsus, chemical republic, Monte Abiegno</keywords>

# PREFACE
## To the Christian and worthy Reader.

Friendly, God-loving Reader, and especially you sons of wisdom and learning, <note>original: "sapientiæ & doctrinæ filij"</note> whereas several years ago, Almighty God—at my constant, fervent prayer and diligent calling—opened my eyes through the illumination of His Holy Spirit (from whom we receive all wisdom, sent to us from the Father through Christ), so far that I recognized the true center in the triangle of the center, <note>original: "centrum in trigono centri"</note> the single and true matter of the noble <term>Philosopher’s Stone</term> <note>The legendary substance capable of turning base metals into gold and granting eternal life</note> and held it substantially in my hands. Yet for nearly five years, I did not know how I should profitably, rightly, and properly separate the <term>Blood of the Red Lion</term> and the <term>Gluten of the White Eagle</term> <note>Alchemical metaphors for fixed and volatile substances, often associated with sulfur and mercury</note> therefrom, much less how to mix them at their best according to the proportioned weight of Nature, to enclose, seal, and commit them to the secret fire, and how to proceed; which must not be done without great reason and practiced caution.

And although I searched with special, notable diligence in the writings, parables, and various figures of the Philosophers, and took great pains to solve their manifold, wonderful riddles—some of which grew out of their own brains—I found in reality <note>original: "reipsa"</note> that it is all together pure fantasy and fool’s work. As the *Aurora Philosophorum* <note>The "Dawn of Philosophers," likely referring to a work attributed to Paracelsus</note> likewise testifies: namely, that all the preparations written of by Geber, Albertus Magnus, their <term>tripods</term>, <term>Athanors</term> <note>A type of furnace used by alchemists designed to maintain a steady heat</note>, reverberating furnaces, melting pots, cementations, distillations, rectifications, circulations, putrefactions, conjunctions, solutions, ascensions, coagulations, calcinations, incinerations, mortifications, revivifications, etc. Just as their tripods, Athanors, reverberating furnaces, melting pots, <term>Sauffmutz</term> <note>An obscure term for a type of vessel or lid</note>, horse dung, ash, sand, <term>cucurbit</term> <note>A gourd-shaped chemical vessel</note>, <term>pelican</term> <note>A circulatory distilling vessel with two side-arms</note>, vial, retort, <term>fixatorium</term>, etc., are pure sophistry, deceptive and useless things. I must also, for my part, confess this in truth: since noble Nature, which very gladly allows herself to be found in her own innate substance, knows nothing of these things.

Therefore <term>Theophrastus</term> <note>Paracelsus (Philippus Aureolus Theophrastus Bombastus von Hohenheim)</note> in his *Secret Magic of the Philosopher’s Stone* <note>original: "Secreto Magico de Lap. Philof."</note> says rightly of those who seek the <term>matter of the stone</term> <note>original: "materiam lapidis"</note> in wine, in imperfect bodies, in blood, in marcasite, in mercury, in sulfur, in urine, in dung, in arsenic, and in herbs such as: celandine, springwort, ivy, hyssop, etc. He says it is all together roguery and thievery by which they lead people astray, cost them money, consume and lose time uselessly and in vain, and only follow their own fool’s heads, which cannot calculate what Nature requires. 

Dear one, tell me: who must burn water for me in the mines of the earth? Or are there people in there who make wine expensive, or children's urine wells with which one makes metals? Or do you think there are apothecaries inside who have all things for sale so that you can make metals? You fool, can you not understand that you err? That none of those things belong to Nature? Or do you want to be above God, that you wish to make metals out of blood? Then make a man out of a horse, or a turnip out of a mouse—that will afterwards give good milk! That would also be a multiplication, but it does not happen. And just as little as that can happen, so little can you make metals from the aforementioned recipes; for the art is not outside of Nature, and where Nature has already

<column-break/>

created a thing, the art can have no effect. When a woman gives birth to a boy, art is not able to make a girl out of him, no matter what thing is used for it. From this, anyone can easily judge how and in what manner, as well as in what, the <term>blessed matter</term> <note>original: "materia benedicta"</note> should be sought and found. 

But let no one imagine, much less be persuaded by any artists, that even if he truly knows the true matter <note>original: "veram materiam"</note>—either through secret revelation from God or from those who know it—and has it in his hands, that he then likewise fully understands how to exclude the same, and knows how to separate and purify the pure from the impure to the highest degree. Oh, my dear laborer, not by far! The dog only now starts licking the buried morsel; art and a skillful mind are required for the matter. Look, for example, at what you heard from me at the beginning: that I knew the true matter of the stone for five years, and during that whole time did not know how to proceed with it, until finally after the sixth year the <term>key of power</term> was entrusted to me through secret revelation from Almighty God. 

And this key the ancient Patriarchs, Prophets, and Philosophers always and at all times kept secret and hidden. For the Monarch says in the mentioned place: if they had written it openly, so that every shoemaker or tooth-puller understood it, that would be a great theft; it would no longer be a secret, and much evil could be committed thereby, which would then be acting openly against the will of God. 

For these reasons, and those I have partly cited in the Epilogue, and so that I should not be seen to bury the talent committed to me by God <note>original: "talentum à Deo mihi commissum"</note>, I have wished to reveal in this, my *Golden Age Reborn*, as much as God and Nature allow, the great secret of the wise, just as my eyes have seen it, my hands have touched it, and which, through the grace of God, I have taken up again at the right time in great power and glory. The pious and God-loving reader will recognize, receive, and accept this in no other way than for good, consider it rightly, and not let himself be misled or troubled by the fact that at times, according to the letter, contradictions occur. In the struggle of theory against practice, it could not and should not have been arranged otherwise: because to write more clearly and plainly of this is seriously and most highly forbidden in the <term>Chemical Republic</term> <note>original: "republica chymica"; the international community of alchemists</note>. 

But I have no doubt that all those who look rightly upon this little treatise in true confidence with the inner eyes of the mind, which can do all things, who study it diligently and above all things call upon God inwardly and from the heart, will, like me, enjoy the hidden Philosophical, wonder-sweet fruits, and become partakers of them according to the will of God. And then they are and remain true brothers of the Golden Cross, and chosen members of the Philosophical community in eternal alliance.

Finally, so that the Christian, sensible, and worthy reader may actually know my Christian name and surname, I will be so candid as to reveal them in the following manner, so that no one can rightfully complain about me: know now everyone, that the number of my name is 1612 <note>original: "M. DCXII."</note>, in which number my whole name is perfectly written in the Book of Nature through 11 dead and 7 living. Beyond that, the 5th letter is the fifth part of the 8th, and the 15th is again the fifth part of the 12th; and let this be enough for you.

->Given at Mount Abiegno, the 25th of March<-
<note>Mount Abiegno, or "Fir Mountain," is a symbolic location in Rosicrucian lore.</note>
->In the year 1621.<-

<summary>This page acts as the title and preface for an alchemical treatise, where the author, Henricus Madathanus, claims to have discovered the secret of the Philosopher's Stone through divine revelation after years of failed experimentation. He critiques mainstream chemical processes as "fool's work" and provides a coded riddle to reveal his true identity to worthy readers.</summary>

<note>original: "AVREVM SECVLVM REDIVIVVM"</note>

### PREFACE
To the Christian and worthy Reader.

Friendly, God-loving Reader, and especially you sons of wisdom and learning <note>original: "sapientiæ & doctrinæ filij"</note>, some years ago, God the Almighty—in response to my constant, fervent prayer and diligent calling upon Him—so far opened my eyes through the enlightenment of His Holy Spirit (from whom we receive all wisdom, sent to us from the Father through Christ) that I recognized the true Center in the Triangle of the center <note>original: "Centrum in Trigono centri"</note>, the unique and true materials of the Noble Philosophical Stone, and actually held them in my hands. Yet for nearly five years I did not know how to separate the Red Lion's blood <note>A metaphor for the "sulfur" or active masculine principle in alchemy.</note> and the White Eagle's gluten <note>A metaphor for the "mercury" or passive feminine principle.</note> from them in a profitable, right, and proper way. Much less did I know how to mix, enclose, and seal them according to the proportioned weight of nature, or how to commit them to the secret fire and proceed <note>original: "committiren, procediren"</note>, which must be done with great reason and practiced caution. And although I searched with special and notable diligence in the writings, parables, and various figures of the Philosophers <note>original: "scriptis, parabolis, & variis Philosophorum figuris"</note>, and took great pains to solve their manifold, wonderful riddles <note>original: "ænigmata"</note>—some of which grew out of their own brains—I found in reality <note>original: "reipsa"</note> that it is all together pure fantasy and fool’s work. As the *Dawn of the Philosophers* <note>original: "Aurora Philosophorum"</note> likewise testifies: that all the preparations <note>original: "præparationes"</note> written of by Geber, Albertus Magnus, and their tripod, Athanor <note>A type of furnace used for slow, uniform heat.</note>, reverberatory furnace, melting furnace, cementations, distillations, rectifications, circulations, putrefactions, conjunctions, solutions, ascensions, coagulations, calcinations, incinerations, mortifications, revivifications, etc.—likewise their tripod, Athanor, reverberatory furnace, melting furnace, caput, horse dung <note>Used as a source of gentle, natural heat.</note>, ash, sand, cucurbit <note>A gourd-shaped chemical vessel.</note>, Pelican vial <note>A circulatory vessel with "arms" that feed condensed liquid back into the body.</note>, retort, fixatory, etc.—are nothing but sophisticated, deceptive, and useless things. I must, for my own part, confess this in truth. For noble Nature, who is quite happy to be found in her own innate substance, knows nothing of these things. Therefore, Theophrastus <note>Theophrastus Paracelsus, the famous Swiss alchemist and physician.</note> in his *Magic Secret of the Philosopher's Stone* <note>original: "Secreto Magico de Lap. Philof."</note> rightly says of those who seek the material of the stone <note>original: "materia riam lapidis"</note> in wine, in imperfect metals <note>original: "imperfecten corporon"</note>, in blood, in marcasite, in mercury, in sulfur, in urine, in dung, in arsenic <note>original: "auripigmento"</note>, and in herbs such as Celandine <note>original: "Chelidonia"</note>, jumping-root, ivy, hyssop, etc.: it is all together roguery and thievery by which they lead people astray, cost them money, consume and lose time uselessly and in vain, and follow only their own foolish heads. They cannot calculate what nature requires. Please tell me: who must burn water for me in the mines of the earth? Are there people inside who make wine expensive, or children's urine springs used to make metals? Or do you think there are apothecaries inside who have everything for sale so that you can make metals? You fool, can you not understand that you are in error? None of these things belong to Nature. Or do you wish to be above God, that you want to make metal out of blood? Then make a human out of a horse, or a turnip out of a mouse, which might then give you good milk—that would also be a multiplication <note>A term for increasing the power or quantity of the alchemical medicine.</note>! But it does not happen. And just as little as that can happen, so little can you make metals from the aforementioned recipes. For the Art is not outside of Nature, and what she has already created

<column-break/>

Art can have no effect upon. When a woman gives birth to a boy, Art is not able to turn him into a girl, no matter what thing is used for it. From this, anyone can easily judge how and in what manner, and in what thing, the blessed matter <note>original: "materia benedicta"</note> should be sought and found. But let no one imagine—much less be persuaded by any artists—that even if he truly knows the true matter <note>original: "veram materiam"</note>, either through the secret revelation of God or from those who know it, and holds it in his hands, that he then likewise knows how to exclude the impurities, and how to separate and purify the pure from the impure <note>original: "purum ab impuro"</note> to the highest degree. Oh, my dear laborer, far from it! "The dog is only now buried in the incense" <note>A German idiom similar to "here's the rub" or "this is where the real difficulty lies."</note>; Art and a skillful mind are required for the matter. Look at me for an example: as you heard at the beginning, I knew the true matter of the stone <note>original: "veram materiam lapidis"</note> for five years, and for that whole time I did not know how to proceed <note>original: "procediren"</note> with it, until finally, after the sixth year, the key of the vault was entrusted to me through secret revelation from Almighty God. And the ancient Patriarchs, Prophets, and Philosophers always kept this key secret and hidden. For, as the Monarch <note>likely referring to Paracelsus</note> says in the cited place: if they had written it openly so that every shoemaker or tooth-puller <note>Terms used to describe unlearned or unqualified practitioners.</note> understood it from then on, it would be a great theft; it would no longer be a secret, and much evil could be prepared through it, which would then be acting openly against the Will of God, etc. For these reasons and those I have partly mentioned in the Epilogue <note>original: "Epilogo"</note>, and also so that I would not be seen to bury the talent entrusted to me by God <note>original: "talentum à Deo mihi commissum"</note>, I have wished to reveal in this, my *Golden Age Reborn*, as much as God and Nature allow, the great Secret of the Wise, as my own eyes have seen it and my hands have grasped it. <note>The following section is a printer's duplication in the original.</note> Also so that I would not be seen to bury the talent entrusted to me by God, I have wished to reveal in this, my *Golden Age Reborn*, as much as God and Nature allow, the great Secret of the Wise, as my own eyes have seen it, my hands have grasped it, and which I have taken out again according to the grace of God at the right time in great power and glory. The pious and God-loving reader will recognize and accept it in no other way than for the good, considering it rightly <note>original: "dextre consideriren"</note>, and will not let himself be led astray or troubled by the fact that at times contradictions <note>original: "contraria"</note> appear according to the letter. It could not have been arranged or allowed otherwise in the struggle of theory toward practice <note>original: "per Theoriam ad praxin"</note>. For to write more clearly and plainly of this is seriously and most highly forbidden in the Chemical Republic <note>original: "republica chymica"</note>. But I have no doubt at all: all those who look upon this little treatise in true confidence with the inner eyes of the mind (which can achieve all things), who study it diligently, and who above all things call upon God fervently and from the heart, will—like me—enjoy the hidden philosophical, wonderful fruits and become partakers of them according to the Will of God. And then they are and remain true Brothers of the Golden Cross and chosen members of the philosophical community in eternal alliance.
Finally, so that the Christian, understanding, and worthy reader may know my actual first and last name, I will be so candid <note>original: "candidus"</note> as to reveal it in the following way, so that no one can rightfully complain about me: know then, everyone, that the number of my name is M. DCXII. <note>1612 in Roman numerals.</note> In which number my whole name has been perfectly written into the Book of Nature through 11 dead and 7 living. Furthermore, the 5th letter is the fifth part of the 8th, and the 15th is again the fifth part of the 12th; and let this suffice for you.
->Given at Mount Abiegnus, the 25th of March<-
<note>original: "Monte abiegno". A symbolic "Fir Mountain" in Rosicrucian lore.</note>
->In the year 1621.<-

# THE GOLDEN AGE REBORN.
<note>original: "AVREVM SECVLVM REDIVIVVM"</note>

<margin>Genesis 30:14.</margin>
As I thought upon the wonders of the Most High, the mystery of locked nature, and the fiery and fervent love of one's neighbor: I remembered the harvest of the Wise, in which Reuben, Leah’s son, found the mandrakes <note>original: "Dudaim"</note> in the field, which were given by Leah to Rachel in exchange for a night with the Patriarch Jacob. My thoughts, however, were very deep and led me further to Moses, namely how he made the Solar Calf cast by Aaron drinkable <note>The author is likening the biblical grinding of the Golden Calf to the alchemical process of creating "potable gold."</note> by burning it with fire, grinding it to powder, strewing it upon the water, and giving it to the children of Israel to drink. I marveled greatly at this man of God’s rapid and skillful destruction of the idol. But when I truly composed my thoughts, I recognized the truth, and my eyes were opened no differently than those of the two disciples at Emmaus, who recognized the Lord in the breaking of the bread. And my heart burned within me, but I lay down to speculate further and fell asleep. And behold, King Solomon appeared to me in a dream with all his power, wealth, and glory. He led with him his entire household of women; <margin>Song of Solomon 6:8.</margin> there were 60 queens, 80 concubines, and of the virgins there was no number. But one was his dove, his pious one, the most beautiful and beloved in his heart. And according to the ancient custom, they held a stately procession, in which the Center was highly honored and loved, whose name was like a poured-out ointment, whose scent surpasses all spices, etc. And his fiery spirit was a key to open the temple, to go into the holy place, and to grasp the horns of the altar.

After the procession was finished, Solomon showed me the unique Center in the Triangle of the Center and opened my understanding. I perceived that behind me stood a naked woman <margin>Song of Solomon 5:1–5</margin> with a bloody wound in her breast, from which blood and water dripped. Her loins, however, stood together like two ornaments made by a master’s hand; her navel was like a round cup that never lacked for drink; her belly like a heap of wheat set about with roses; her two breasts like two young twin fawns; her neck like an ivory tower; her eyes like the pools in Heshbon by the gate of Bath-rabbim; her nose like a tower on Lebanon looking toward Damascus; her head stood upon her like Carmel, and the hair upon her head was like royal purple bound in folds. But her clothes, which she had cast off, lay at her feet; they were quite hideous, stinking, and poisonous. And she began to speak: "I have taken off my robe; how shall I put it on again? I have washed my feet; how shall I <margin>Song of Solomon 5:3, 7</margin> soil them again? The watchmen who go about the city found me; they struck me, wounded me, and took away my veil." Then I trembled out of fear and lack of recognition, and I fell to the earth. But Solomon bade me stand up again and said: "Do not be afraid, for you see uncovered Nature and the most secret thing that is under heaven and upon earth. She is beautiful as Tirzah, lovely as <margin>Song of Solomon 6:4</margin> Jerusalem, terrible as an army with banners, and yet she is the pure, chaste virgin from whom Adam was made and created. Truly, the entrance to her pavilion is sealed and hidden, for she dwells in the gardens and sleeps in the double cave of Abraham on the field of Ephron, and her palace is in the depths of the Red Sea and in transparent cliffs. She was born of joy and raised by fire; therefore, she is a queen of the land. She has milk and honey in her breasts; yes, her lips are like dripping honeycomb; honey and milk are under her tongue, and <margin>Song of Solomon 4:11</margin> the scent of her garments is to the Wise like the scent of Lebanon, but to the ignorant, it is an abomination." And Solomon spoke further: "Take heart, look

<column-break/>

at my entire household of women and seek one like her." And immediately the entire household of women had to politely disrobe. I searched, but my thoughts could not judge, and my eyes were held so that I did not recognize her.

When Solomon noticed my weakness, he separated his household of women from this naked woman and said: "Your thoughts are vain, and your understanding is burned by the sun, and your memory is as black as fog, so that you cannot judge rightly. However, if you do not throw your affairs away and will take heed of the present opportunity, this naked virgin’s bloody sweat and snow-white nourishment can refresh you again, purify your understanding and memory, and fully restore them, so that your eyes may recognize the great works <note>original: "magnalia"</note> of the Most High. You shall truly investigate the height of the uppermost, the depth of the lowermost, and the foundation of all nature, and the power and effect of all elements. Your understanding will be like silver, and your memory like gold; the colors of all precious stones will appear before your face, and you will know their birth, and separate the good from the evil, the goats from the sheep. Your life will be rest, but the bells of Aaron shall waken you from sleep, and the harp of David my father from slumber." At this speech of Solomon, I was even more terrified and was beyond measure shocked, partly because of the commanding words, and partly because of the great pomp and glory of the present royal household of women. And King Solomon took me by the hand and led me through a wine cellar into a secret but very stately hall, where he refreshed me with flowers and comforted me with apples; <margin>Song of Solomon 2:5</margin> its windows were of transparent crystal, and I looked through them. He spoke: "What do you see?" I answered: "I see from this room into the previous one from which I came; and your royal household stands to the left, and the naked virgin to the right. Her eyes are redder than wine, her teeth whiter than milk, but her clothes at her feet are more hideous, blacker, and more foul than the Brook Kidron." "Choose one of them all," said Solomon, "to be your beloved. I value her and my household equally; and as much as the loveliness of my ladies delights me, so little do her repulsive clothes terrify me." And immediately the King turned and spoke quite friendly with one of his queens. There was an old, hundred-year-old matron <note>original: "Hoffmeisterinne"</note> among them who wore a gray robe and a black cap on her head set with snow-white pearls, lined inside with red taffeta, and embroidered with blue and yellow silk, very skillfully stitched through. Her cloak was enhanced with all kinds of Turkish colors and Indian figures. This old woman gave me a secret wink and swore a solemn oath that she was the mother of this naked virgin; that the girl was born of her body—a chaste, pure, and hidden virgin who until now would not suffer the gaze of any man. And although she allowed herself to be used among the people everywhere in the streets, yet no man before this had ever seen her naked nor had a single one touched her, for she was the virgin of whom the prophet says: "Behold, we have a secret born son, who is transformed among others; behold, the virgin has given birth," such a virgin who is called **Apdorossa**, that is, the hidden one, who cannot suffer another. Because this daughter of hers was still unmarried, she had the bridal treasure lying under her feet because of the present danger of war, so that she might not be robbed by a marauding band and deprived of her stately wealth. But I should not let myself be deterred by the disgust of the unsightly clothes, but rather choose her daughter before all others for my <margin>Song of Solomon 2:6</margin> love and life’s delight. Then she would give me and reveal to me a lye to clean her clothes, and I would obtain a liquid salt and an inextinguishable oil for my household, and an inexhaustible treasure; and her right hand would constantly embrace me, <margin>Song of Solomon 8:3</margin> and her left hand would always lie under my head. And as I was about to...

<summary>The author experiences a visionary dream where King Solomon reveals a naked, wounded woman representing "Nature" or the alchemical prime matter. An old matron reveals the woman is named Apdorossa and offers her as a bride, promising alchemical secrets like liquid salt and inextinguishable oil.</summary>
<keywords>alchemy, King Solomon, Song of Solomon, Apdorossa, nature, prime matter, tincture, vision, allegory</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the allegorical dream sequence. In the previous page, the narrator reflected on Moses and the Golden Calf before falling into a deep, meditative sleep. Here, he enters a visionary dialogue with King Solomon and a mysterious elderly woman—recurring figures in alchemical literature representing Wisdom and Nature.</meta>

...to explain it categorically, Solomon turned around again, stared at me, and said: "I am the wisest on Earth; beautiful and delightful is my harem, and the magnificence of my queens surpasses the gold of Ophir. The jewelry of my concubines overshadows the rays of the sun, and the adornment of my virgins the moonlight. Thus heavenly are my realms, unfathomable my wisdom, and inexhaustible my understanding." Then I answered and bowed, half-terrified: "Behold, if I have found grace before you, since I am poor, give me this naked virgin whom I have chosen among all others for the preservation of my life. Her clothes are indeed soiled and torn, but I will cleanse her and love her from the heart, and she shall be my sister, my bride, because with one of her eyes and with one of her neck-chains, she has taken my heart and made me so fervent that I lie sick with love." And immediately Solomon handed her over to me, and there was such a commotion in the harem that I awoke from it, and did not know what had happened to me. Nevertheless, I took it for a dream and had all sorts of subtle thoughts about it until morning. But when I stood up and had said my prayer, behold: I saw the naked virgin's clothes lying before my bed, but not her. I began to feel anxious and fearful, and all my hair stood on end, so that cold sweat ran down my whole body. Yet I took heart, recalled my dream, and reflected upon it in the fear of the Lord; however, my thoughts were not able to fathom it. For these reasons, I did not dare to inspect the clothes, much less to recognize anything in them, but instead changed my bedroom and, for a long time, <note>original: "ex mera tamen ignorantia"</note> out of sheer ignorance, let the clothes lie there, thinking that if I were to perhaps touch them or turn them over, something remarkable might happen to me. However, in my sleep, I had been heavily poisoned and inflamed by the stench of the clothes, so that my eyes could not see the time of grace, nor my heart recognize the great wisdom of Solomon.

But after the aforementioned clothes had lain in my bedroom for five years, and I did not know what use they were, I finally thought to burn them to clear the lodging. And as I spent the whole day occupied with such thoughts, the hundred-year-old woman appeared to me the following night in a dream and addressed me quite harshly: "You ungrateful human! For five years now I have entrusted to you my daughter’s clothes, among which her most noble jewels are hidden, and in all that time you have neither cleaned them nor aired them out for the sake of the moths and worms. And beyond all that, you now finally want to burn the clothes with fire? Is it not enough that you are a cause of the death and downfall of my daughter?" Then I became somewhat hot under the collar <note>literally "hot in the forehead"</note> and gave her as an answer: "How should I understand this? Do you wish to make a murderer out of me? In five years, I have not seen your daughter with my eyes, nor heard the least thing from her; how then can I be a cause of her death?" And she would not let me finish speaking, but said: "It is all true, only you have sinned against God; therefore my daughter could not be granted to you, nor could the Philosophical Lye I promised for washing and cleaning her clothes follow. For when you began—when Solomon willingly gave you my daughter and you had a loathing for her clothes—the planet Saturn, who is her grandfather, grew angry, and out of pure wrath transformed her back into what she had been before her birth. Thus, through your disdain, you have angered Saturn and caused her death, decay, and final downfall. For she is the very one of whom Senior <note>Senior Zadith, an influential 10th-century Arabian alchemist often cited in European texts.</note> says:
> Oh woe! Bring me a naked woman, for unsightly was my first body, and I had never yet become a mother, until I was born a second time. Then I gave birth to the power of the roots of all herbs; in my essence, I became victorious, etc."
Such heartbreaking words and the like seemed very strange to me, yet I restrained my temper as much as was humanly possible. Nonetheless, I solemnly protested that I knew nothing of her daughter, much less of her death, decay, and downfall. It was true that I had kept her clothes in my bedroom for five years, but because of great blindness, I had not recognized them, nor could I imagine their use, and therefore I should be innocent before God and all men. This my rightful, well-founded excuse did not please the old mother poorly; she looked at me and said: "I perceive and note from your sincere conscience that you are innocent, and your innocence shall be richly and well rewarded. Therefore, I will reveal to you out of a good heart and in secret: that my daughter, out of special love and the affection she bore for you, left you a gray marbled casket as an inheritance among her remaining clothes, which is covered with a coarse, black, filthy lining." (And with that, she gave me a glass with lye and spoke further.) "That same casket you shall clean well of the stench and filth it received from the clothes. You will find therein two things: a white silver box full of magnificent and polished diamonds ground on lead—

<column-break/>

—and also a golden piece adorned with precious Solar Rubies. And this is the treasure and the entire estate of my late daughter, all of which she bequeathed and left to you as an inheritance before her transformation and passing. If you will hereafter combine this treasure with one another, purify it to the highest degree and in silence—yet with great patience—lock it in a warm, hidden, steamy, transparent, and moist cellar, and protect it from frost, wind, hail, sudden lightning, hot thunderbolts, and other external destruction until the wheat harvest, only then will you first feel and partake in the great glory of the inheritance." And with that, I awoke once more, and behold, I called upon God anxiously, that He might open my understanding to find the casket that was promised and pledged to me in the dream. After finishing my prayer, I searched with the greatest diligence and desire among the clothes and found it; but the lining was so hard-locked around it and grown into it by nature that I was not able to get it off. For it would neither be cleaned with the lye nor allow itself to be split with iron, steel, or other metal. So I let it stand once more and did not know what I should do with it; I held it for a work of sorcery and thought of the saying of the prophet: <margin>Jeremiah 2:22</margin> "For though thou wash thee with nitre, and take thee much soap, yet thine iniquity is marked before me, saith the Lord God."

And another year passed, during which I still did not know how to remove the lining despite speculating and diligent reflection, until I finally went walking in a garden to drive away melancholic thoughts. After long <note>original: "deambuliren"</note> strolling about, I sat down upon a stone chest <note>likely a stone bench or a sarcophagus used as a garden seat</note> and fell fast asleep over it. <margin>Song of Solomon 5:2</margin> "I slept, but my heart was awake." Then the old hundred-year-old governess appeared to me again and said: "Have you received my daughter's estate?" I answered with a sad voice: "No, I have indeed found the casket, but to separate the lining from it is still impossible for me at this time; the lye you gave me will not attack the lining." At this simple speech of mine, the old woman began laughing and said: "Do you want to eat mussels and crabs with the shells? Must they not first be ripened and prepared by the ancient cook-planet, Vulcan?" <note>Vulcan is the Roman god of fire, used here to represent the chemical heat required for alchemical transformation.</note> "I said you should clean the gray casket to the highest degree with the gifted lye that sprang from it, and not the outward raw lining; rather, you must first burn that same lining with the Fire of the Wise; only then will it be made ready." And immediately following this, she gave me several glowing coals wrapped in white silk <note>original: "Zindel"</note>, with further instruction and indication that I should make a philosophical and very skillful fire from this and burn the lining; then I would soon find the gray casket. And instantly there arose <margin>Song of Solomon 4:16</margin> a North and South wind; both blew together through the garden. At this I awoke, cleared my eyes of sleep, and became aware that the glowing coals wrapped in white silk lay at my feet. I seized them hastily and with joy, prayed diligently, called upon God, studied and labored day and night, thinking meanwhile of the magnificent and excellent saying of the philosophers where they say: <note>original: "Ignis & azoth tibi sufficiunt"</note> "Fire and Azoth are sufficient for you." Of this, Esdras also says in the fourth book: <margin>2 Esdras 14:39</margin> "And it reached me a cup full of fire, and its appearance was like fire, and I drank it; then wisdom grew in me. And God gave me the fifth understanding, and my spirit was preserved in memory, and my mouth was opened and no longer closed. And when forty nights were past, 204 books were finished, seventy solely for the wisest, and those were worthy to be read and were written on boxwood."

And I proceeded thus <note>original: "in silentio & spe"</note> in silence and hope, as the old mother had revealed to me in the dream, until after a long time following Solomon's promise, my understanding became silver and my memory golden. But after I had duly and quite skillfully installed and locked away the treasure of her daughter according to the instruction and teaching of the old governess—namely: the magnificent, shining Lunar Diamonds and the Solar Rubies, both of which were found and sprang from one casket and one landscape—I heard the voice of Solomon, which said: <margin>Song of Solomon 5:10-16</margin> "My friend is white and red, chosen from among many thousands. His locks are curly, black as a raven. His eyes are like doves' eyes by the water brooks, washed with milk, and fitly set. His cheeks are like the growing spice-beds of the apothecaries. His lips are like roses dropping with flowing myrrh. His hands are like golden rings full of turquoise. His body is pure like ivory, adorned with sapphires. His legs are like marble pillars set upon golden feet. His form is like Lebanon, choice as the cedars. His throat is sweet and altogether lovely. Such is my friend, my friend is such a one, O daughters of Jerusalem: therefore you shall hold him <margin>Song of Solomon 3:4</margin> and not let him go, until you bring him into his mother's house, into his mother's chamber." And when Solomon had finished these words, I did not know how to answer them, but became silent; nonetheless, I intended to open the enclosed treasure again, so that I might find peace...

<summary>This page concludes an alchemical allegory where the narrator, guided by King Solomon and a personification of Nature (the old woman), learns to process the "inheritance" of a naked virgin. Through the "Fire of the Wise" and the cleaning of a symbolic casket, he achieves a state of enlightened understanding and begins the final stage of the Great Work.</summary>

# THE GOLDEN AGE
## REBORN.

<margin>Genesis 30:14</margin>
As I thought upon the wonders of the Most High, the mysteries of locked Nature, and the fiery and fervent love of my neighbor, I remembered the Harvest of the Wise, in which Reuben, son of Leah, found the mandrakes <note>original: "Dudaim"</note> in the field, which were given by Leah to Rachel in exchange for sleeping with the patriarch Jacob. My thoughts, however, were very profound and led me further to Moses, namely how he made the Solar Calf cast by Aaron into something potable <term>potable; drinkable gold was considered a high-level alchemical medicine</term> by burning it with fire, grinding it into powder, strewing it upon the water, and giving it to the children of Israel to drink. I marveled greatly at this man of God’s swift and artistic destruction; but when I truly composed my thoughts, I recognized the truth, and my eyes were opened no differently than the two disciples at Emmaus, who recognized the Lord in the breaking of bread. And my heart burned within me, but I lay down to speculate further and fell asleep over it. And behold, King Solomon appeared to me in a dream with all his power, wealth, and glory, leading with him his entire harem: there were 60 queens and 80 concubines, and of the virgins there was no number; but one was his dove, his pious one, the most beautiful and beloved in his heart. And according to the Catholic custom <note>The author uses "Catholic" here in its original sense of "universal" or "grand"</note> they held a stately procession, in which the Center was highly honored and loved, whose name was like a poured-out ointment, whose scent surpasses all spices, etc. And his fiery spirit was a key to open the temple, to enter into the holy place, and to seize the horns of the altar.

<margin>Song of Solomon 6:8</margin>
<margin>7:1–5</margin>
After the procession was completed, Solomon showed me the single Center in the Triangle of the center <note>original: "Trigono centri"</note> and opened my understanding. I became aware that behind me stood a naked female figure with a bloody wound in her breast, from which blood and water dripped. Her loins stood together like two ornaments made by the master’s hand, her navel was like a round goblet that never lacked drink, her belly like a heap of wheat set about with roses, her two breasts like two young twin roes, her neck like an ivory tower, her eyes like the pools in Heshbon by the gate of Bath-rabbim, her nose like a tower upon Lebanon looking toward Damascus, her head stood upon her like Carmel, and the hair upon her head was like the King's purple bound in folds. Her clothes, however, which she had cast off, lay at her feet; they were quite hideous, stinking, and poisonous, and she began to speak:

<margin>Song of Solomon 5:3, 7</margin>
"I have taken off my robe; how shall I put it on again? I have washed my feet; how shall I soil them again? The watchmen who go about the city found me, they struck me, wounded me, and took away my veil." Then I was struck with fear and ignorance and fell to the earth. But Solomon bade me stand up again and said: "Do not be afraid, for you see Nature stripped bare, and the most secret thing that is under heaven and on earth."

<margin>Song of Solomon 6:4</margin>
"She is beautiful as Tirzah, lovely as Jerusalem, terrible as an army with banners, and is nonetheless the pure chaste Virgin from whom Adam was made and created. Truly, the entrance to her hut is sealed and hidden, for she dwells in the gardens and sleeps in the double cave of Abraham on the field of Ephron, and her palace is in the depths of the Red Sea and in transparent clefts. She has poured out much pleasure and raised up the fire; therefore she is a queen of the land. Milk and honey are in her breasts,"

<margin>Song of Solomon 4:11</margin>
"yes, her lips are like dripping honeycomb; honey and milk are under her tongue, and the scent of her clothes is to the Wise like the scent of Lebanon, but to the ignorant an abomination." And Solomon spoke further: "Rouse yourself, look

<column-break/>

at my entire harem, and seek her equal." And immediately the entire harem had to politely strip themselves bare; I searched, but my thoughts could not judge, and my eyes were held so that I did not recognize her.

But when Solomon noticed my weakness, he separated his harem from this naked female figure and said: "Your thoughts are vain and your understanding is scorched by the sun, and your memory is black as fog, so that you cannot judge rightly. However, if you do not throw your affairs away and will take heed of the present opportunity, this naked virgin’s bloody sweat and snow-white tears can refresh you again, purify your understanding and memory, and fully restore them, so that your eyes may recognize the great works <note>original: "magnalia"</note> of the Most High. You shall truly investigate the height of the uppermost, the depth of the lowermost, and the foundation of all Nature, and the power and effect of all elements. Your understanding will be of silver, and your memory of gold; the colors of all precious stones will appear before your face, and you will know their birth, and separate the good from the evil, the goats from the sheep. Your life will be rest, but the bells of Aaron will awaken you from sleep, and the harp of David my father from slumber." At this speech of Solomon, I was even more terrified and was exceedingly dismayed, partly because of the heart-breaking words and partly because of the great pomp and glory of the present royal harem. And King Solomon took me by the hand, led me through a wine cellar into a secret but very stately hall, wherein he refreshed me with flowers and comforted me with apples; its windows, however, were of transparent crystal, and I looked through them. He spoke: "What do you see?" I answered: "I see from this room into the previous one from which I came, and your royal harem stands to the left, and the naked virgin to the right side; her eyes are redder than wine, her teeth whiter than milk, but her clothes at her feet are hideous, blacker, and more unsteady than the Brook Kidron." "Choose one of them all," said Solomon, "as your beloved. I value her and my harem equally, and as much as the loveliness of my ladies delights me, so little do her loathsome clothes terrify me." And immediately the King turned and spoke quite friendly with one of his queens. There was an old hundred-year-old governess among them who had a gray robe on, a black cap on her head set with snow-white pearls and lined inside with red taffeta, embroidered with blue and yellow silk and quite artistically sewn through; her mantle was raised with all sorts of Turkish colors and Indian figures. This old woman gave me a secret wink and swore a heavy oath that she was the mother of this stripped virgin; that she was born of her body, a chaste, pure, and hidden virgin, who until now had refused to suffer the gaze of any man. And although she had allowed herself to be used among the peoples everywhere in the streets, yet no man before this had ever seen her naked nor had a single one touched her, for she was the virgin of whom the Prophet says: "Behold, we have a secret born son, who is transformed among others; behold, the virgin has given birth," such a virgin who is named Apdorossa, <note>This name is likely a variation of "Aphthartos" (incorruptible) or a coded alchemical name for the Materia Prima</note> that is, "the secret one who cannot suffer another." Because her daughter was still unmarried, she had the bridal dowry lying under her feet because of the current danger of war, so that she might not be robbed by a marauding troop and deprived of her stately wealth. I, however, should not let myself be deterred by the disgust of the unsightly clothes, especially since her daughter was above all others for my love and life's delight; then she would give and reveal to me a lye to clean her clothes, and I would obtain a liquid salt and an incombustible oil for my household, and an inexhaustible treasure,

<margin>Song of Solomon 2:6, 8:3</margin>
and her right hand would constantly embrace me, and her left hand would constantly lie under my head. And as I hereupon...

<summary>In this allegorical dream, King Solomon introduces the narrator to a naked, wounded woman who represents pure Nature (Materia Prima). An elderly governess, acting as Nature's mother, promises the narrator that he can gain divine wisdom and alchemical treasures—liquid salt and incombustible oil—if he looks past the woman's filthy exterior to cleanse and marry her.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the alchemical allegory. Having previously reflected on the biblical Moses and the Golden Calf, the narrator now engages in a visionary dialogue with King Solomon. He is presented with the "Materia Prima" (the first matter of alchemy) in the form of a ragged, naked maiden, symbolizing the crude state of nature before it is purified into the Philosopher's Stone.</meta>

...to explain categorically, Solomon turned around, stared at me,
and spoke: "I am the wisest on Earth; beautiful and delightful is my 
harem, and the magnificence of my queens surpasses the gold 
of Ophir. The jewelry of my concubines overshadows the rays of the 
Sun, and the adornment of my virgins the moonlight. Thus heavenly are my 
ladies, unfathomable my wisdom, and inexhaustible my understanding."
Then I answered and bowed, half-terrified: "Behold, if I have found 
grace before you, since I am poor, give me this naked maiden 
whom I have chosen among all others for the preservation of my life. 
Her clothes are indeed soiled and torn, but I will clean them and 
love her from my heart. She shall be my sister, my bride, because 
with one of her eyes and one of her necklaces, she has captured 
my heart and made me so fervent that I lie sick with love." 
And immediately Solomon handed her over to me, and there was such a 
commotion in the harem that I awoke from it. I did not know what 
had happened to me; nonetheless, I held it for a dream and had 
all sorts of subtle thoughts about it until morning. But when I stood up 
and performed my prayer, behold: there I saw the naked maiden's clothes 
lying before my bed, but she was not there. I began to feel 
anxious and fearful, and all my hair stood on end, so that cold sweat 
ran down my entire body. Yet I took heart, recalled 
my dream, and pondered it in the fear of the Lord; however, 
my thoughts were unable to fathom it. For these reasons, 
I did not dare to inspect the clothes, much less 
to recognize <note>original: "recognosciren"</note> anything in them. Instead, I changed my bedroom 
and for a long time—*out of pure ignorance* <note>original Latin: "ex mera tamen ignorantia"</note>—left the clothes 
lying there, thinking that if I perhaps touched or turned them, 
something remarkable might happen to me. However, in my sleep, I was 
heavily poisoned and inflamed by the stench of the clothes, so that my eyes 
could not see the time of grace, nor could my heart recognize the great 
wisdom of Solomon.

But after the aforementioned clothes had lain in my bedroom for five years 
and I did not know what use they were, I finally thought 
to burn them to clear the lodging. And as I had spent and passed 
the entire day with such thoughts, the old hundred-year-old woman appeared 
to me in a dream the following night and spoke to me 
quite harshly in this manner: "You ungrateful human! For five years now, 
I have entrusted my daughter's clothes to you, among which her 
most noble jewels are hidden, and all this time you have neither 
cleaned them nor aired them out to keep away the moths and 
worms. And above all that, you now finally want to burn the clothes 
with fire? Is it not enough that you are a cause of the death and 
downfall of my daughter?" Then I became a bit hot under the collar and 
gave her an answer: "How should I understand that? Do you want to 
make a murderer out of me? In five years, I have not seen 
your daughter with my eyes, nor heard the slightest thing about her. 
How then can I be a cause of her death?" And she would not let me 
finish speaking, but said: "It is all true, except that you have sinned 
against God; therefore my daughter could not be granted to you, 
nor could the Philosophical Lye <term>a symbolic chemical wash used to purify matter</term> I promised for washing 
and cleaning her clothes follow. For when you began, when Solomon 
willingly gave you my daughter and you felt a loathing for her clothes, 
the planet Saturn—who is her grandfather—grew angry and, 
out of sheer rage, transformed her back into what she had been before 
her birth. Thus, through your disdain, you have angered Saturn 
and caused her death, decay, and final downfall. For she is indeed 
the one of whom Senior <note>Senior Zadith, a famous 10th-century Arabian alchemist</note> says: 'Alas! Bring me a naked woman, for 
my first body was primordial, and I had never become a mother 
until I was born a second time; then I brought forth the power 
of all herbs' roots; in my being I became victorious,' etc." 
Such and similar heart-breaking words seemed very strange to me, yet 
I restrained my zeal as much as was humanly possible; nevertheless, 
*I solemnly protested* <note>original Latin: "protestirte ich solenniter"</note> against it, saying that I knew nothing of 
her daughter, much less of her death, decay, and downfall. It was true 
that I had kept her clothes in my bedroom for five years, but 
because of great blindness, I had not recognized them nor could I 
imagine their use, and therefore I was innocent before God and 
all people. This my rightful, well-founded excuse did not 
displease the old mother. She looked at me and said: "I perceive 
and notice from your sincere conscience that you are innocent, and 
your innocence shall be richly and well rewarded. Therefore, out of 
a good heart and in secret, I want to reveal to you: that my daughter, 
out of singular love and affection for you, left a gray, 
standardized little box <note>original: "Normaliertes Kästlein," likely a specific alchemical vessel</note> as an inheritance among her remaining clothes, 
which is covered with a coarse, black, filthy lining." (And with that, she 
gave me a glass of lye and continued speaking): "You shall clean that 
same box from the stench and filth it received from the clothes. 
You will need no key; rather, it will open itself, and 
you will find two things inside: a white silver boykin <note>likely a small vessel or statue</note> full 
of magnificent diamonds, ground and polished upon lead, 

<column-break/>

and also a golden piece adorned with precious Solar Rubies <term>symbolizing the "Red" stage of alchemical perfection, or Gold</term>. 
And this is the treasure and the entire estate of my late 
daughter, all of which she bequeathed and left to you as an inheritance 
before her transformation and passing. If you now artfully 
combine this treasure, purifying it to the highest degree and, in silence, 
yet with great patience, lock it away in a warm, hidden, steamy, 
transparent, and moist cellar, and protect it from frost, wind, 
hail, swift lightning, hot thunder-rays, and other external 
destruction until the White Harvest, only then will you truly feel 
and partake in the great magnificence of the inheritance." 
And at that point, I awoke once more, and behold, I called 
anxiously upon God to open my understanding to seek 
the little box promised and pledged to me in the dream. After 
finishing my prayer, I searched the clothes with the greatest diligence 
and desire and found it. But the lining was so tightly closed around it 
and grown to it by nature that I was unable to get it off. For it 
would neither be cleaned with the lye, nor allow itself to be split 
with iron, steel, or any other metal. So I let it stand again 
and did not know what to do with it; I held it for a work of magic 
and thought of the saying <margin>Jeremiah 2:22</margin> of the Prophet: "For though you wash yourself with lye 
and use much soap, yet your iniquity is marked before me, 
says the Lord God."

And another year passed, during which, despite *speculating* 
and diligent reflection, I did not know how to *remove* <note>original Latin: "remouiren"</note> the lining. 
Until finally, to drive away melancholic thoughts, I went to *stroll* <note>original Latin: "spacirn"</note> 
in a garden. After a long *walk* <note>original Latin: "deambuliren"</note>, however, I sat down on a 
Kissing Stone <note>A traditional term for a stone where one rests</note> and <margin>Song of Solomon 5:2</margin> fell fast asleep over it. "I slept, 
but my heart was awake." Then the old hundred-year-old governess 
appeared to me again and said: "Have you received my daughter's 
inheritance?" I answered with a sad voice, "No; I have indeed 
found the box, but separating the lining from it is still 
impossible for me at this time. The lye you gave me will not 
attack the lining." Upon this my simple speech, the old 
woman smiled and said: "Do you want to eat mussels and crabs 
with their shells? Must they not first be ripened and prepared 
by the ancient planet-cook Vulcan <term>symbolizing alchemical fire</term>? I told you 
that you should clean the gray box to the highest degree with the 
gifted lye that sprang from it—and not the external raw lining. 
Instead, you must first burn that lining with the Fire of the Wise; 
only then will it be made ready." And immediately she gave me 
several glowing coals wrapped in white silk <note>original: "Zindel," a thin taffeta</note>, 
with further instruction and indication that I should make a 
Philosophical and quite artful fire from them and burn the lining; 
thus I would soon find the gray box. And suddenly <margin>Song of Solomon 4:16</margin> a North 
and South wind arose; both blew together through the garden. 
I awoke from this, wiped the sleep from my eyes, and became 
aware that the glowing coals wrapped in white silk lay at my 
feet. I seized them hastily and with joy, prayed diligently, 
called upon God, studied and worked day and night, and in the 
meantime thought of the magnificent and excellent saying of the 
philosophers, where they say: *Fire and Azoth are sufficient for you* 
<note>original Latin: "Ignis & azoth tibi sufficiunt." Azoth is the universal solvent.</note>. 
Of this Esdras also says in the fourth book: <margin>2 Esdras 14:39</margin> "And he gave me a 
cup full of fire, and its appearance was like fire, and I drank it. 
Then wisdom grew in me: And God gave me the fifth understanding, 
and my spirit was preserved in memory, and my mouth was opened 
and was no longer shut. And when 40 nights had passed, 204 books 
were completed—70 of them only for the wisest, and those were 
worthy to be read and were written on boxwood <note>original: "Buybaum"</note>." 
And so I *proceeded* *in silence and hope* <note>original Latin: "procedirte... in silentio & spe"</note> as the 
old mother had revealed to me in the dream, until a long time 
after Solomon's promise, my understanding became silver and my 
memory became golden. After I had properly and quite skillfully 
installed and locked away the treasure of her daughter according to 
the instruction and teaching of the old governess—namely: the 
magnificent shining Lunar Diamonds and the Solar Rubies, which both 
sprang from and were found in one box and one landscape—I heard 
<margin>Song of Solomon 5:10</margin> the voice of Solomon saying: "My friend is white and red, 
the choice one among many thousands. His locks are curly, 
black as a raven. His eyes are like doves' eyes by the 
water-brooks, washed with milk, and they are fully set. 
His cheeks are like the growing spice-beds of the apothecary. 
His lips are like roses that drip with flowing myrrh. 
His hands are like golden rings full of turquoise. His body is 
pure like ivory, adorned with sapphires. His legs are like 
marble pillars founded on golden feet. His appearance is like 
Lebanon, choice as the cedars. His throat is sweet and 
altogether lovely. Such a one is my friend; my friend is <margin>Song of Solomon 3:4</margin> 
such a one, you daughters of Jerusalem. Therefore you shall hold 
him and not let him go until you bring him into his mother's house, 
into his mother's chamber." And when Solomon had finished 
speaking these words, I did not know how to answer him, but 
rather became mute. Yet I intended to open the enclosed treasure 
again, so that I might find peace...

<summary>The narrator receives a symbolic "dirty maiden" from King Solomon, representing the raw material of alchemy. After five years of neglect, a maternal figure (Nature) instructs him to use the fire of Vulcan to burn away the outer "filth" to reveal a box of silver and gold jewels.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the alchemical allegory. The narrator, having previously reflected on the biblical Moses and the Golden Calf, now experiences a visionary dream involving King Solomon. This vision serves as a transition from biblical meditation to the specific alchemical mystery of the "Materia Prima" (the first matter), personified as a naked, wounded maiden who must be purified.</meta>

## Epigram
### To the sons of Wisdom and Learning.

> What I sought, I have found; I have purified it many times; and
> I have joined it; I have matured it: a golden Tincture followed,
> which is called the center of Nature: from there
> come so many meanings, so many writings of men, and various figures.
> I frankly confess to all, it is a MEDICINE for metals;
> and for the infirm at the same time: a point born of the divine.
<note>original: "QUæ siui inueni: purgaui sæpius..." The author describes the alchemical process: purification, conjunction, and maturation to create the "Tincture" or Philosopher's Stone.</note>

->HARMANNUS DATICHIVS,<-
->Servant of the Author.<-

# THE GOLDEN AGE RESTORED.

<margin>Genesis 30:14.</margin> As I meditated upon the wonders of the Most High, upon the mystery of hidden Nature, and upon the fiery and fervent love of my neighbor, I remembered the harvest of the wise, in which Reuben found the <term>mandrakes</term> <note>original: "Dudaim"; biblical plants believed to aid fertility and possess magical properties</note> in the field, which were given by Leah to Rachel in exchange for a night with the patriarch Jacob. My thoughts, however, were very deep and led me further to Moses: how he made the <term>Solar Calf</term> <note>The Golden Calf from Exodus</note> cast by Aaron <term>potable</term> <note>drinkable; a reference to "Aurum Potabile" or drinkable gold, a legendary alchemical life-elixir</note> by burning it with fire, grinding it into powder, strewing it upon the water, and giving it to the children of Israel to drink. I marveled greatly at this man of God’s swift and skillful destruction of the idol. But as I truly grasped my thoughts, I recognized the truth, and my eyes were opened no differently than those of the two disciples at Emmaus, who recognized the Lord at the breaking of bread. And my heart burned within me; I laid myself down to speculate further and fell asleep. And behold, King Solomon appeared to me in a dream with all his power, wealth, and glory. He led his entire harem with him: <margin>Song of Solomon 6:8.</margin> there were 60 queens and 80 concubines, and of the virgins there was no number. But one was his dove, his pious one, the most beautiful and dearest in his heart. And according to <term>Catholic custom</term> <note>Likely referring to a "universal" or "stately" religious ceremony rather than specifically Roman Catholicism</note> they held a magnificent procession, in which the Center was highly honored and loved; its name was like a poured-out ointment, whose scent surpasses all spices, etc. And its fiery spirit was a key to opening the Temple, to enter into the Holy Place, and to grasp the horns of the Altar.

After the procession ended, Solomon showed me the single Center in the <term>Triangle of the center</term> <note>Trigono centri; a geometric symbol of alchemical balance</note> and opened my understanding. I perceived that behind me stood a naked female figure with a bloody wound in her breast, from which blood and water <margin>Song of Solomon 5:1–5.</margin> trickled. Her loins were joined like two clasps made by the hand of a master; her navel was like a round goblet that never lacked for drink; her belly was like a heap of wheat set about with roses; her two breasts like two young twin roes; her neck like an ivory tower; her eyes like the pools in Heshbon by the gate of Bath-rabbim; her nose like the tower of Lebanon looking toward Damascus; her head sat upon her like Carmel, and the hair upon her head was like the King’s purple tied in folds. But her clothes, which she had cast off, lay at her feet; they were entirely hideous, stinking, and poisonous. And she began to speak: "I have taken off my robe; how shall I put it on again? I have washed my feet; how shall <margin>Song of Solomon 5:3, 7.</margin> I soil them again? The watchmen who go about the city found me; they beat me, they wounded me, and they took away my veil." Then I started with fear and lack of recognition and fell to the ground. But Solomon bid me stand up and said: "Do not be afraid, for you see naked Nature, the most secret thing that is under heaven and upon earth. <margin>Song of Solomon 6:4.</margin> She is beautiful as Tirzah, lovely as Jerusalem, terrible as an army with banners; and yet she is the pure, chaste virgin from whom Adam was made and created. Indeed, the entrance to her hut is sealed and hidden, for she dwells in the gardens and sleeps in the double cave of Abraham on the field of Ephron, and her palace is in the depths of the Red Sea and in transparent clefts. The fire bore her, and the fire raised her; therefore she is a queen of the land. She has milk and honey in her breasts; yes, her lips are like a dripping honeycomb; honey and milk are under her tongue, <margin>Song of Solomon 4:11.</margin> and the scent of her garments is to the wise like the scent of Lebanon, but to the ignorant, an abomination." And Solomon spoke further: "Cheer up, look

<column-break/>

at my entire harem, and seek her equal." And immediately the entire harem had to politely strip themselves. I searched, but my thoughts could not judge, and my eyes were held so that I did not recognize them.

When Solomon noticed my weakness, he separated his harem from this naked female figure and said: "Your thoughts are vain, and your understanding is scorched by the sun, and your memory is as black as mist, so that you cannot judge rightly. However, if you do not ruin your affairs and will take heed of the present opportunity, this naked virgin’s bloody sweat and snow-white tears can refresh you again, purify your understanding and memory, and restore them perfectly, so that your eyes may recognize the <term>magnalia</term> <note>great works or wonders</note> of the Most High. You shall truly investigate the height of the uppermost, the depth of the lowermost, and the foundation of all Nature, the power and effect of all elements. Your understanding will be as silver, and your memory as gold; the colors of all precious stones will appear before your face, and you will know their birth, and separate the good from the evil, the goats from the sheep. Your life will be rest, but the bells of Aaron shall awaken you from sleep, and the harp of David my father from slumber." At these words of Solomon, I was even more terrified and was exceedingly dismayed, partly because of the piercing words, and partly because of the great splendor and glory of the royal harem present. And King Solomon took me by the hand and led me through a wine cellar into a secret but very magnificent hall, where he refreshed me with flowers and comforted me with apples; its <margin>Song of Solomon 2:5.</margin> windows, however, were of transparent crystal, and I looked through them. He spoke: "What do you see?" I answered: "I see from this room into the previous one from which I came; and your royal harem stands on the left, and the naked virgin on the right side. Her eyes are redder than wine, her teeth whiter than milk, but her clothes at her feet are more hideous, blacker, and more unsteady than the Brook Kidron." "Choose one of all," said Solomon, "as your beloved. I regard her and my harem as equal; and as much as the loveliness of my ladies delights me, so little do her repulsive clothes terrify me." And immediately the King turned and spoke very kindly with one of his queens. There was an old hundred-year-old governess among them who wore a gray robe and a black cap on her head set with snow-white pearls, lined inside with red taffeta, embroidered with blue and yellow silk, and very skillfully sewn through. Her cloak was raised with all sorts of Turkish colors and Indian figures. This old woman gave me a secret wink and swore a solemn oath that she was the mother of this naked virgin; that the girl was born of her body, a chaste, pure, and hidden virgin who until now would not suffer the gaze of any man. And although she had allowed herself to be used among the people everywhere in the streets, yet no man before this had ever seen her naked nor had a single one touched her. For she was the virgin of whom the Prophet says: "Behold, we have a secret born son, who is transformed among others; behold, the virgin has given birth, such a virgin whose name is <term>Apdorossa</term> <note>A symbolic name. The text explains it as "secret" or "one who cannot endure others." It likely stems from the Greek 'apdoros' or a coded alchemical term for the pure Materia Prima.</note>, which means: secret, she who cannot endure another." Because this daughter of hers was still unmarried, she had the bridal dowry lying under her feet for the sake of the current danger of war, so that she might not be robbed by a marauding band and deprived of her magnificent wealth. I, however, should not let myself be deterred by the horror of the unsightly clothes, but choose her daughter before all others for my love and life's delight. Then she would give me and reveal to me a lye to clean her clothes; thus I would obtain a liquid salt and an incombustible oil for my household, and <margin>Song of Solomon 2:6.</margin> an inexhaustible treasure; and her right hand would always embrace me, <margin>Song of Solomon 8:3.</margin> and her left would always lie under my head. And as I hereupon...
