---

> To him who overcomes, I will give to eat of the hidden manna, and will give him a white stone, and on the stone a new name written, which no one knows except him who receives it. Revelation 2 v. 17.

Christ is the trunk and Tree of Life, through which the bitter water at Marah was sweetened, and we are His branches and boughs, in and through which He brings forth fruit by His power within us. From the bitterness of this temporal life, He has reborn what had faded back into the sweetness of eternal life.

His soul is the property of my soul, and it was for the sake of the same that God became human, so that we—in and through Him—might be <term>deified</term> <note>original German: "vergötter." The concept of *theosis*, or the soul becoming "god-like" through union with Christ.</note> again in the love and life of Jesus Christ, and be reborn and revealed as an eternal heavenly image, to a divine life. O man, it is a great, unfathomable mystery when we rightly consider and remember that in Christ we are members of one body, and in Him we are all only One. That we all enjoy and receive the unique Christ, and in that same Christ, be and remain only one single body—which is He Himself—within His members. Yes, we are linked and bound with and in Him into a single man, and that same single man is everyone in Christ himself. We all eat and drink Christ's flesh and blood under bread and wine (as through a medium) with the essential desire and the hungry mouth of faith; understand this as the fiery mouth of love and life of the soul. The true inner man—who is a Paradisiacal and eternal being, the image of God, and hidden under the animal coarseness—desires a spiritual <term>tincture</term> <note>original German: "Tinctur." In alchemy, a tincture is a potent substance that transforms and ennobles; here, it refers to the spiritual essence of Christ's blood.</note> or nutriment (for he is himself a spiritual being) and eats Christ's spiritual flesh and blood.

The soul’s hunger of faith is the divine spiritual mouth that eats and drinks Christ’s flesh and blood in the inward ground of the mind. That which proceeds from the Spirit and consists in the Spirit is the highest and greatest nutriment of the soul. The spiritual, hungry soul-fire grasps, seizes, eats, and drinks in its spiritual mouth of faith—opened in full desire—the holy, incomprehensible essence, Christ’s flesh and blood, into its soul-mouth: the supernatural, holy, essential power under bread and wine as an ordained medium. Through this medium, the invisible Eternal gives itself to the visible, spiritual, immortal, eternal man; understand that faith becomes one essence in such a mixing, as a spiritual flesh and blood. For one faith catches the other: the earnest faith of the living catches the eternal, and is so mighty and powerful that it can overturn and move mountains. For the true, essential faith in man is Christ Himself, who is and remains in him, and is his life and light.

See, thus we are purified through faith and illumined and enlightened through the Holy Spirit. The coarse animal, which is only a husk, receives only an elemental being or food, and eats from the perishable worldly essence from which it came; but the soul eats from God and His Word, from which it came. Note: as the mouth is, so also is the food. Every <term>Principle</term> <note>referring to the three planes of existence (divine, astral, and elemental) common in Boehmean and Rosicrucian philosophy.</note> eats and drinks of its likeness. Every spirit eats and drinks of that from which it originated, and in whose ground it stands in its center. For what fellowship has light with darkness? It cannot comprehend it.

The natural, mortal, and corruptible body receives its nourishment from the earth; and the <term>sidereal</term> <note>original: "syderische." The "astral" body influenced by the stars/heavens in Hermetic thought.</note>, fleeting, and perishable body its nourishment from the firmament; but the Spirit of the Lord is the life of the soul, and the life of the inner immortal life.

The inner man, as the pure Adamitic body of power, along with his beautiful, chosen, gracious, lovely, heavenly bride, receives—in great desire of love—the invisible spiritual essence and supernatural heavenly flesh of Christ into his soul-mouth of faith. This is a tincture of life, a penetrating fiery essence of love and power; for faith is the foundation, the beginner and the perfecter, who works the true inward everlasting Sabbath: the power of God, through the love of Jesus, in still rest and gentle bliss, in the inner ground of the soul within us.

Christ did not give His disciples the creaturely, external, tangible, fleshy humanity, or the formed being—no; he who does not distinguish the Lord's body from the bread and wine receives it to his own judgment. He gave them the spiritual humanity, the power of His body and blood, a holy, heavenly body-life and loveliness, a spiritual being, a spiritual body which He brought from heaven. In this, the divine and eternal human power is understood; He leads this into our body, which has faded from the light, so that He might make it alive through His heavenly essence within it.

No perishable creature can behold a spiritual being, much less can we grasp, seize, and enjoy such a thing with our earthly, mortal mouth. But to the spirit of the soul, which stands in the divine center, it is graspable and comprehensible; for He, Christ, is the body of the spirit and the spirit of the body—the body of our soul, which He gives us to eat in faith. No mortal and unworthy man can or will receive or attain this, much less taste the unchangeable and incomprehensible Bread of Heaven of eternity.

The <term>Dew of Heaven</term> <note>a metaphor for the universal spirit or "mercury" captured from the air</note> and the fatness of the earth is our artistic subject or <term>Materia</term>. It is therefore neither mineral nor metal; the Pythagorean Y shows us that there are two mercurial substances of one root, fire and water, <term>Ischschamaim</term> <note>from Hebrew: Esh (fire) and Shamayim (heavens)</note>, namely <image-desc>Mercury symbol emerging from a 'Y' shape labeled 'Minera'</image-desc> drawn from the <term>Minera</term> <note>the "ore" or raw source</note> in which all metals and minerals lie. It is a <image-desc>Mercury symbol</image-desc> Dew of Heaven, but a mineral and metallic Dew of Heaven, in which all the colors of the world lie, which may be coagulated through art into a sweet salt, called <term>Manna</term>, for medicine; <term>Sol Pater, Luna Mater</term> <note>Sun the Father, Moon the Mother</note>, from these two it receives its light, life, and brilliance: from the Sun its fiery light-essence, and from the Moon its watery light-essence. We find it both coagulated and dissolved. This dew falls from above into the depths of the earth, and its body is from the most subtle part of the earth, while its soul and spirit, fire and light, come from above and enter into a salty body, receiving the powers (namely the <term>Virtutes Substantiales</term> <note>substantial virtues or essential powers</note>) of things above and below. To our eyes, this mineral dew appears in the colors white, yellow, green, red, and black; according to the outer eyes, it has no more colors than these. For it appears to the outer eyes corporally; in the mountains, it is sometimes seen by the miners, thick and water-dripping to the outer eyes, but it is useful neither to them nor to the artists, since one cannot know for what purpose it was ordained by nature—whether for a mineral or a metal, and of what kind; that remains unknown and unrecognizable. The best is the coagulated kind, like <term>Electrum</term> <note>an alloy of gold and silver, or amber-like substance</note>, or like transparent amber, in the color as mentioned. I do not know what the world uses it for, and yet it is with its powers in all things. But it is itself despised and rejected by them; it divides into two branches, white and red, from the single root Y, and it stands on the single root where it grows, like a white and red <term>Rose of Jericho</term>, and blooms like a lily standing in the <term>Valley of Jehoshaphat</term>; often broken off prematurely by the miner, or martyred by ignorant workers. The true artist notices its influence and plucks it himself in its ripeness, with blossom, seed, root, stem, and branches, namely in its ripeness through the vision of the inner, opened eyes. Let this be enough regarding its bodily form; it is neither metal nor mineral, and yet the initial mother and first matter of all metals and minerals.

-> It is nothing but the <term>Lion</term> with his coagulated blood, and the <term>Gluten of the White Eagle</term>. <-

# THE BOOK OF LIFE IS CHRIST
<note>original Latin: LIBER VITÆ CHRISTUS</note>
### or
### The only true way to come to the true and righteous knowledge of God and nature, and to true perfection.

-> Riddle. Enigma. <-

**Our** (the truly faithful's) residence is imprisoned in a black-gray dark castle, so enchanted and surrounded by a very thick cloud that no one can reach it. Before this castle, and around it, is a beautiful green meadow; upon this lies a hard rock, which prevents the castle from being seen; upon this rock, a square pillar of alabaster is erected, in which a golden scepter, exquisitely set with many precious stones, is stuck crosswise. Down from the rock goes a staircase of 11 steps made of white marble; around the entire building flows a wide, deep water, at whose shore lies a powerfully equipped ship covered with blue velvet; the master of the same, along with his servants, is dressed in long red crimson-satin cloaks, and they stand bound at anchor. Not far from there springs a living fountain, by which a pyramid with an obelisk is placed, on which the buildings of this adventurous island are described at length in 12 languages. If one wishes to reach the princely castle and discover it, one must first pass through a dilapidated tower called the "Uncertain Passage," from which the situation of the dark and invisible castle can be measured and viewed; then one comes to another tower, called the "Dangerous One," which one must pass on foot, and which also presses across the water up to the rock; and when one touches the scepter with the middle finger, and knightly overcomes and slays the wolf and he-goat that one encounters without reason, then an exceedingly beautiful maiden appears and honors the victor with a small wreath as a testimony of his virtue and bravery. And immediately the clouds vanish, and the castle presents itself; the commander therein shows himself in a yellow long silk robe and a high brown beret, receives the new guest, and leads him to all earthly and heavenly bliss.

<summary>This page describes the "Dew of Heaven" (Mercurial spirit) as the primary material of alchemy, emphasizing its dual nature and spiritual origin. It concludes with an allegorical riddle describing the difficult journey of the soul or the alchemist toward enlightenment and the "Castle" of perfection.</summary>

<keywords>Mercury, Dew of Heaven, Ischschamaim, Minera, Philosopher's Stone, Rose of Jericho, Valley of Jehoshaphat, Lion, Eagle, Book of Life, Riddle, Enigma</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the author's mystical and reform-minded exhortation, moving from the internal nature of faith to a call for universal transformation through the "revelation" of Jesus Christ. It addresses different social classes—the nobility, the clergy, and the common people—urging a shift from outward religious practice to an inward reality of love.</meta>

# Of the Revelation of Jesus Christ.

->**LOVE**<-
->**INRI** <note>original Latin: "Iesus Nazarenus, Rex Iudaeorum," meaning "Jesus of Nazareth, King of the Jews."</note><-
->**IHS** <note>A sacred monogram for the name of Jesus, derived from the first three letters of His name in Greek.</note><-
->**FAITH** — **HOPE**<-

Dear Christian people: just as Jesus Christ, the Son of God, was revealed by the Holy Spirit through His Father (as the Father is revealed by the Son), and shall be revealed further, then not only will those who only claim to be Christians become true Christians, but all nations shall accept the Christian faith and become Christian people. But until now, as can clearly be seen, Jesus Christ and the Christian faith have never truly been revealed; for the love of one's neighbor and the love of God are entirely closed off and extinguished. In their place rules "the flesh"—that is <note>original: "i. e."</note>, idolatry, adultery, drunkenness, gluttony, envy, anger, strife, discord, murder, theft, injustice, and all vanity and ambition. All of this is entirely contrary to the revelation of Jesus Christ and against His new creation <term>new birth: a spiritual rebirth or transformation of the soul</term>. Oh, what miserable people we are to accuse ourselves, yet still be so set against one another! Why do we bite and quarrel with each other? Has the love that was poured out through the Holy Spirit been so completely extinguished and died out? Does no one respect the other? Are we then irrational animals, that one tears apart, devours, and shames the other? Was heaven and earth created for only one person? Oh, no.

O! You nobles, counts, knights, and noblemen, it is necessary that we pray to God the Creator for the revelation and knowledge of Jesus Christ His Son, who is mocked, scorned, and despised, and yet is the greatest in heaven and on earth.

O you theologians and clergy, together with all teachers: you should be taught by God, and you should proclaim Jesus Christ of Nazareth, the Crucified, to all people with right knowledge and teaching. You must do this out of love, and not for the sake of money or gain. It is also necessary for you to pray to God the Almighty for the revelation and the right, true knowledge of Jesus Christ, the Son of God and of the Virgin. Then you may truly precede the people as lights of this world, provided you have enlightenment from God. Learn to know Jesus Christ, who is made unto us wisdom, and righteousness, and sanctification, and redemption, so that, as it is written: "He that glorieth, let him glory in the Lord" <note>original Latin reference: 1. Cor. 1. v. 31.</note>. Then all strife, quarreling, condemning, and labeling others as heretics <term>verketzern: to denounce someone as a heretic, often leading to social or legal persecution</term> will be entirely abolished. Furthermore, brotherly love and unity shall be among you, with friendliness, of one mind, one will, one knowledge, one faith, one salvation, and the entirely perfect love of neighbor and of God.

O all people in this world, rich and poor, man and woman, young and old, great and small: let us pray to God for the revelation and right true knowledge of Jesus Christ, His Son. Thus we shall live united, peaceful, friendly, patient, gentle, and humble of heart among one another. We shall be one heart with all people in the whole world, one love, one community, one church, of one faith and mind in Jesus Christ, through whom we and all people are saved.

Otherwise, we do not yet rightly know Jesus Christ the Crucified. No one should boast of Him with the mouth alone, for He looks at the heart; where love is, all injustices that are committed in the whole world fall away.

O lovely, sweet, loving Love, Love of all love, what a sweet yoke you are! Thus speaks the heavenly and eternal Wisdom and Love itself. <note>References to Matthew 11 and John 3.</note>

**Vocabulary:** Jesus Christ, Revelation, Faith, Love, Hope, Holy Spirit, Knowledge, Righteousness, Redemption, Virgin, Unity, Salvation.

<summary>This page serves as a passionate plea for a true, internal "revelation" of Christ that manifests as universal love and social harmony, rather than mere dogma or outward piety. The author critiques the vices of his time and calls on all social classes—from nobles to clergy—to abandon strife and greed in favor of spiritual unity.</summary>

--- Page 104 ---
<meta>This page continues the theological discourse on the nature of Christ, shifting from abstract definitions to a practical call for universal Christian love and the "true revelation" of Jesus in the heart of the believer.</meta>

# On the Revelation of Jesus Christ.

<note>The following image describes a symbolic map of the soul's virtues.</note>
[A circular diagram centered on the upper half of the page. At the top of the circle is a heart containing the word "LOVE" and a small crucifix with "INRI" <note>Latin: Iesus Nazarenus, Rex Iudaeorum; "Jesus of Nazareth, King of the Jews"</note>. In the center is a radiant sunburst bearing the "IHS" <note>A traditional monogram for the name of Jesus</note> monogram. Flanking the center are two larger crosses: the left is labeled "FAITH" and the right is labeled "HOPE". Lines interconnect these symbols within a double-lined circular border.]

Dear Christian people: if Jesus Christ, the Son of God, has been revealed by the Holy Spirit through his Father—just as the Father is revealed by the Son—and continues to be revealed, then not only will so-called Christians become true Christians, but all peoples will accept the Christian faith and become Christian people. 

But until now, as can clearly be seen, Jesus Christ and the Christian faith have never been truly revealed; for the love of neighbor and the love of God are entirely locked away and extinguished. In their place rule the flesh—that is <note>original: "i. e.", id est</note>, idolatry, fornication, drunkenness, gluttony, envy, anger, strife, quarreling, murder, theft, injustice, and all vanity and ambition. All of this is entirely contrary to the revelation of Jesus Christ and against his new <insert>birth</insert> creature. <note>The author uses "creature" here in the sense of a "new creation" or a transformed human being.</note> Oh, why do we miserable people accuse one another so! Why do we bite and quarrel with each other? Is love, which is poured out through the Holy Spirit, entirely extinguished and dead? Does no one respect another? Are we then irrational animals, that one tears apart, devours, and shames the other? Were heaven and earth created for one person alone? Oh, no.

O! You nobles, counts, knights, and gentlemen, it is necessary that we pray to God the Creator for the revelation and knowledge of Jesus Christ His Son, who is despised, mocked, and scorned, and yet is the greatest in heaven and on earth.

O you theologians, clergy, together with all teachers: you should be taught by God, and you should proclaim Jesus Christ of Nazareth, the Crucified One, to all people with right knowledge and teaching. This must be done out of love, and not for the sake of money or profit. You also need to pray to God the Almighty for the revelation and right, true knowledge of Jesus Christ, the Son of God and of the Virgin. Only then may you truly walk before men as lights of this world, provided you have enlightenment from God. Learn to recognize Jesus Christ, who is made for us as wisdom, righteousness, sanctification, and redemption; so that, as it is written: "Let him who boasts, boast in the Lord." <note>1 Corinthians 1:31</note>. Then all strife, quarreling, condemning, and denouncing others as heretics <term>Verketzern: the practice of labeling someone a "Ketzer" (heretic) to exclude them from the community</term> will be entirely abolished. Furthermore, brotherly love and unity will be among you, with friendliness, being of one mind, one will, one knowledge, one faith, one salvation, and entirely perfect love of neighbor and of God.

O all people in this world, rich and poor, man and woman, young and old, great and small: let us pray to God for the revelation and right, true knowledge of Jesus Christ, His Son. Then we will live among one another in unity, peace, friendliness, patience, gentleness, and heartfelt humility. We will be one heart with all people in the whole world—one love, one community, one church, of one faith and mind in Jesus Christ, through whom we and all people shall be saved.

Otherwise, we do not yet truly know Jesus Christ the Crucified. No one should boast of him with the mouth alone, for he looks upon the heart; where love is, all injustices in all things committed in the whole world fall away.

O lovely, sweet, loving Love, Love of all love, what a sweet yoke you are! Thus speaks the heavenly and eternal Wisdom and Love itself. <note>Matthew 11; John 3</note>

[A small illustration of a flaming heart pierced diagonally by an arrow.]

**Vocabulary:** Revelation, Jesus Christ, Love, Faith, Hope, Knowledge, Righteousness, Unity, Heart, Cross, INRI, IHS.

<summary>A passionate appeal for universal Christian unity and brotherly love, arguing that true "revelation" is found in character and compassion rather than in dogmatic quarreling or greed.</summary>

--- Page 106 ---
<meta>This page features a mystical prayer of devotion and submission, likely influenced by the theosophical writings of Jacob Boehme, as evidenced by the specific terminology regarding "Something" (Jchts) and "Nothing" (Nichts).</meta>

Lord, from your hand comes everything good; all blessing and <term>benediction<note>original: "Benedeiung" — an archaic German term for a formal blessing or hallowing.</note></term> descends from your hand. You have written the <term>signature of nature<note>original: "Character der Natur" — This refers to the "Signatura Rerum," the mystical belief that God has inscribed a hidden "handwriting" or character into the physical forms of all created things, revealing their inner essence.</note></term> with your fingers; no one can read it unless they have been in your school. Therefore, just as servants look toward the hands of their masters, and maids toward the hands of their mistresses, so too do our eyes look toward you until you help us, Lord our God. Who should not praise you? Who should not laud you, O King of Honor? For everything is from you and belongs to you alone, and everything must flow back to you again, either into your love or your wrath. Nothing can escape your grasp; everything must serve your glory and majesty. You alone are Lord and no one else; you do what you will with your mighty arm. No one can run away from you. You alone help the humble, the meek, and the poor—those devoted to you from the heart—out of their distress; to those who humble themselves before you in the dust and earth, you are gracious. Who should not praise you, O King of Honor, whom no one equals, whose dwelling is in heaven and in a <note>The original "geängsteten" suggests a heart "constricted" or "troubled" by spiritual trial or contrition, similar to the "contrite heart" of the Psalms.</note>troubled and virtuous divine heart. O great God: you, the all in all! O Nature! You, an <term>Aught<note>original: "Jchts" — A specific mystical term, often spelled "Ichts," used to describe the "Something" or the divine manifestation that emerges out of the primordial "Nothing" (Nichts).</note></term> out of Nothing, what more can I say? I am Nothing in myself, an Aught in you, and I live in your Aught out of Nothing; live you then in me, and bring me out of the Aught in you. Amen.

<summary>This page contains a profound mystical prayer acknowledging God as the author of the "signature of nature" and the source of all existence. It emphasizes the movement of the soul from "Nothing" to becoming "Something" (Aught) through divine grace.</summary>
<keywords>Signatura Rerum, Jacob Boehme, Ichts and Nichts, King of Honor, divine providence, mysticism, contrition, Holy Spirit</keywords>

> Proverbs 27, v. 7.
> A full soul treads underfoot even a honeycomb, but to a hungry soul every bitter thing is sweet.

<column-break/>

> Likewise at 14, v. 6.
> A scoffer seeks wisdom and finds it not, but knowledge is easy for him who understands.

The almighty, uniquely wise, and omniscient God and Lord has endowed man with reason above all other animals, so that he should recognize His work and not leave it unexamined. Thus, the man whom the omniscient God has awakened to this high and deeply hidden work and the great secret of the ancient <term>Water-Stone of the Wise</term><note>A term for the Philosopher's Stone, emphasizing its "mercurial" or liquid state during the process.</note> should rightly take it upon himself. For if there is any natural thing in the world, then the preparation and the <term>Magisterium</term><note>The "Mastery" or the specific technical process of the alchemical work.</note> of the philosophical stone is natural; it is not the work of a man, but entirely the work of Nature herself. For the artist adds nothing to it, except only that he directs Nature into growth, just as every husbandman does with his fruits and plantings. He must only be subtle in understanding and have the grace of God to govern its course, as the work reveals itself successively in the cooking and through time: namely, in the beginning, the <term>Subjectum</term><note>The "Subject" or raw material, often called the Materia Prima.</note>—which one receives into one's hand from Nature—wherein the <term>Universal Tincture</term><note>A potent liquid or powder that can transform any substance it touches.</note> of all metals, animals, and plants lies hidden. It is an unformed body; it has neither the shape nor form of any animal or plant, but is at first a rough, earthy, heavy, slimy, tough, and misty-watery essence, at which point Nature has stopped. 

But when the enlightened man opens this matter, examines it in the <term>Digestion</term><note>The process of heating a substance gently over a long period.</note>, and purifies its thick-misted shadow with which it is surrounded, and lets the hidden thing crawl forth, and through further <term>Sublimation</term><note>The process of turning a solid into vapor and back to solid to purify it.</note> also divides from it its inner soul which is hidden therein, and brings it into a corporeal essence—then one finds what Nature had hidden in such a previously unformed shape, and what power and <term>Magnalia</term><note>Great wonders or divine virtues.</note> the highest Creator has implanted and bestowed in this creation. For God implanted such power in this creation above all other creatures from the very beginning of creation, and still endows them daily. Were that not so, it would be impossible for any man on earth to bring such a natural work to a desired end, let alone to create any single use within it. But the charitable and good God

<column-break/>

does not at all begrudge man the treasures and the goods that He has implanted in Nature; otherwise, He would not have bestowed such things upon the creatures, but created everything for man’s benefit and made man the master over His creation. Therefore, it is right for man to recognize and also to attain such a natural philosophical work; otherwise, such a highly endowed and wonderful creation would have been placed in our midst in vain, and we would pass it by like cows unobservant of Nature, and God’s counsel would go unfulfilled, and Nature would not reach her final end. <term>God and Nature do nothing in vain.</term><note>original: "Deus autem & Natura, nihil faciunt frustra." A common scholastic and alchemical maxim.</note> But Almighty God holds the governance in all such things, ordering and making it so that oats and fodder are poured out for the donkey and horse, but more precious and lovely food is presented to the rational man. Therefore, those who desire to look into and fathom such a deeply hidden <term>Secret</term><note>original: "Arcano"</note> and high treasure in a proper manner must not turn to the carping of the ignorant, for they have no understanding in the light of Nature.

However, the <term>Philosophers</term> and wise men—both the <term>Moderns</term><note>original: "Neoterici"</note> and the <term>Ancients</term><note>original: "Veteres"</note>—have disputed in various ways regarding this secret art. With many different names, parables, and wonderful, strange, sophisticated words, they have indicated the <term>Subjectum</term> and its <term>Essence</term>: what kind of <term>Matter</term>, what kind of <term>Body</term>, what kind of <term>Subjectum</term>—indeed, what a wonder-thing and secret creature it may be, in which such powerful, wonderful, and heavenly strength is incorporated. After its digestion and purification, one can help men, animals, plants, and metals, bringing them to the highest degree of health and perfection, and accomplish much else besides. Thus, all who have been and still are true <term>Philosophers</term> have, with one accord through interchangeable speeches and writings, wished to point out and show only one single <term>Goal</term><note>original: "Scopum"</note> and one single <term>Matter</term> to the <term>Sons of Wisdom</term><note>original: "Filii Sapientiae"</note>. Here, however, regarding the essence, there is a great silence—

<summary>This page introduces the alchemical "Great Work" as a natural process ordained by God, requiring both human reason and divine grace to transform raw, "misty" matter into the perfected Philosopher's Stone. The author defends alchemy against scoffers, arguing that God intended for humans to master the secrets hidden within nature.</summary>
<keywords>Philosopher's Stone, Materia Prima, Digestion, Sublimation, Universal Tincture, Light of Nature, Sons of Wisdom, Alchemical Gateway, Reason vs. Ignorance</keywords>

Almighty, alone wise, and all-knowing God and Lord has gifted man with reason above all other animals, so that he may recognize His work and not leave it unexamined. Thus, the person whom the all-knowing God has awakened for this purpose should rightfully take up this high and deeply hidden work—the great secret of the ancient <term>Water-Stone of the Sages</term> <note>original: "Wasser-Stein der Weisen"; a specific name for the Philosopher's Stone emphasizing its mercurial or "watery" origin</note>. For if there is any natural thing in the world, it is the preparation and the <term>Magisterium</term> <note>the "mastery" or perfected process</note> of the Philosophical Stone. It is natural and not the work of man, but entirely and completely the work of Nature herself; for the artist adds nothing to it, except that he directs Nature toward growth, just as every plowman does with his fruits and plantings. He must only be sharp-witted in understanding and have the grace of God to govern its course as the work reveals itself in the cooking and successively through time.

Namely, at the beginning, the <term>Subjectum</term> <note>the starting material</note> which one receives from the hand of Nature—within which the universal tincture of all metals, animals, and plants lies hidden—is an unrefined <term>Corpus</term> <note>body</note>. It has neither the shape nor form of any animal or plant, but is at first a rough, earthly, heavy, slimy, tough, and misty-watery essence, at which point Nature has stopped. But when the enlightened man opens this matter, seeks it out through <term>Digestion</term> <note>the process of gentle, continuous heating</note>, and purifies its thick-misted shadow with which it is surrounded, he lets the hidden thing crawl forward. Through further sublimation, he also divides from it its internal soul that was hidden within, bringing it into a bodily essence. Then one finds what Nature had previously hidden in such an unrefined shape, and what power and great wonders the highest Creator has implanted and bestowed upon this created thing. For God implanted such power into this creature above all other creatures from the very beginning of creation, and He still gifts it daily; were that not so, it would be impossible for any man on earth to bring such a natural work to its desired end, much less to create a single nut herein. But the benevolent, good God, who

<column-break/>

does not at all begrudge man the treasures and the riches that He has implanted in Nature; otherwise, He would not have bestowed such things upon His creatures, but created everything for the good of man, and made man the master over His creation. Therefore, it is fitting for man to recognize and also to attain such a natural philosophical work; otherwise, such a highly gifted and wonderful creature would have been placed in our midst in vain. We would pass by Nature without consideration, like cows, and God's counsel would go unfulfilled, and Nature would not reach her final end. *God and Nature do nothing in vain.* <note>original Latin: "Deus autem & Natura, nihil faciunt frustra."</note> However, Almighty God holds the governance in all such things, ordering and arranging them so that oats and fodder are scattered for the donkey and the horse, but more precious and lovely food is presented to the rational human being. Therefore, those who desire to look into and fathom such deeply hidden <term>Arcano</term> <note>secrets or mysteries</note> and high treasures in a proper manner must not heed the carping of the ignorant, for they have no understanding in the Light of Nature.

Indeed, the <term>Philosophi</term> <note>Philosophers</note> and wise men, both the <term>Neoterici</term> <note>the moderns</note> and the <term>Veteres</term> <note>the ancients</note>, have disputed in various ways concerning this secret art. With many different names, parables, and wonderfully strange, sophisticated words, they have hinted at the <term>Subjectum</term> <note>the subject/starting matter</note> and its <term>Essentia</term> <note>essence</note>—what kind of <term>Materia</term> <note>matter</note>, what kind of <term>Corpus</term> <note>body</note>, what kind of starting material, indeed, what a wondrous thing and secret creature it may be, in which such powerful, miraculous, and heavenly strength is embodied. By following its <term>Digestion</term> and purification, one can help humans, animals, plants, and metals, bringing their health and perfection to the highest degree, and accomplishing much else with it. Yet all who were and still are true Philosophers have unanimously, though with varying speech and writings, intended to point and show the <term>Filii Sapientiae</term> <note>Sons of Wisdom; a traditional term for alchemy students</note> toward only one single <term>Scopum</term> <note>goal or target</note> and one single Matter. Here, however, concerning the essential nature, there is a great Still-

<summary>This page introduces Part III of the work, framing alchemy as a natural process ordained by God where the "artist" (alchemist) acts merely as a guide to Nature, similar to a farmer. It describes the starting material of the Philosopher's Stone as a raw, "slimy" substance that must be purified to reveal its inner soul and medicinal power.</summary>
<keywords>Water-Stone of the Sages, Magisterium, Universal Tincture, Arcanum, Subjectum, Essence, Digestion, Sons of Wisdom, Natural Law, Alchemy as Agriculture</keywords>

--- Page 113 ---
<meta>The previous page likely discussed the secrecy of the Great Work and the origins of the materials used by the alchemists. This page continues the warning against revealing the "Subject" (the raw material) to the unworthy.</meta>

...silence, having placed a padlock on their own mouths and pressed a firm seal upon them; for if it were as common as brewing or baking, the world would perish.

This single "Thing," however, which <note>original: "solvit se ipsum, coagulat se ipsum, se ipsum impraegnat, mortificat & vivicat"</note> dissolves itself, congeals itself, impregnates or moistens itself, kills and brings itself to life again, has had many seekers. Yet most have failed, having lost their way in the search. For it is a thing that is closest to gold; and it is such a thing that the poor as well as the rich can obtain it, wherever it may be found. But the Philosophers threaten divine execration and call down the curse of God upon anyone who expressly names the <term>Subjectum</term> <note>The 'Subject' refers to the raw, secret starting material used to create the Philosopher's Stone.</note> with his mouth.

Which curse of the Philosophers the Almighty God also upholds, granting their petition, as He has kept it under His hand for several thousand years now. This is how it stands with the aforementioned Subject: for our <term>Magnesia</term> <note>In alchemical texts, 'Magnesia' is a cryptic name for the 'primordial matter' or the 'root' of the metals, not the modern chemical magnesium.</note> contains not only a small, proportional quantity of the universal <term>Life Spirit</term> <note>original: "Spiritus Vitalis"</note>, but is so well condensed and made compact by heavenly power that many have become intoxicated by its vapor. It lies there in its place and can no longer help itself. If a wise man, knowledgeable in such matters, comes upon it—be it in the depths of a mountain or wherever else he may find it—he takes a bed-full of that juice. For by the special and abundant grace of God, both poor and rich are free to reach for it. He then goes homeward to his house, behind the stove or into another chamber where it pleases him to be comfortable, and begins to build and labor with it. He can keep it so NIMBLY that even his own household will not notice it. For this natural work does not proceed as sloppily as the messy work of common laborers—such as coal-burning, smelting, refining, and the like—but is a work that one can keep in a locked chest in whatever room he wishes, provided only that no cold comes upon it. And if necessity requires, he can carry on his trade quite well alongside it, provided only that he knows how to set the furnace (which must be of triple-proven strength) with the right heat, and lets Nature take its course.

When the earthiness has been removed from it through <term>Solution</term> <note>The process of dissolving a solid into a liquid state.</note>, and it has been sharpened through long <term>Digestion</term> <note>A process of slow, gentle heating over a long period, similar to incubation.</note>, stripped of its crude matter, prepared to the most subtle degree and reborn—and afterwards, the high-born, sharp, and powerful Spirit is many times added to it again, like an influence or nourishment, by way of <term>Imbibition</term> <note>A process of 'feeding' the matter by adding liquid drop by drop until it is absorbed.</note> and nutrition at certain times in a proper quantity—then its power is condensed over the aforementioned matter. When new "pillars" from its brothers are added and driven into it daily, how do you think such a work might be brought forth? For such power and immeasurably hidden strength of the Life Spirit comes to the crude matter or "Subject" from the stars and the constellations of heaven in its earth. From this, the <term>Secret Spirit of the Universe</term> <note>original: "Spiritus universi secretus"</note> of the Philosophers is drawn, which is the <term>Mercury of the Wise</term>. It is the beginning, the middle, and the end, in which is enclosed and hidden the <term>Physical Gold</term> <note>original: "Aurum Physicum"</note>, which common laborers vainly expect to extract from common gold. Because the Philosophers deal much with <term>Sol and Luna</term> <note>Alchemical names for Gold (the Sun) and Silver (the Moon).</note> in their writings—which are the most stable metals in 🜂 <note>Alchemical symbol for Fire</note>—this is not to be understood literally. For their Sol and Luna, when brought to their internal purity and reconciled through the right, natural, proper, and philosophical preparation, are like the heavenly stars of the Sun and Moon, which illuminate day and night, the upper and lower firmament with their clarity. Therefore, these two noble metals—as the Sol and Luna of the Philosophers—being by nature like the human body, can pour in such great health to those who use them rightly and know how to prepare them. Beyond this, there is nothing else to prepare but the single, threefold point of the Universal. It is the Spirit, enclosed in these two, which is productive and works such constancy, power, and virtue as is found in no other things.

Since the man graced by God can now arrange and prepare a thing from the aforementioned Red or White, or Sol and Luna—which is called the <term>Philosopher's Stone</term> <note>original: "Lapidem Philosophorum"</note> or the ancient Water-Stone of the Wise—from a creature in which God placed such power at the creation of the world, or implanted such matter or "Subject" for the love and pleasure of the highly gifted men of God. I hold, however, that the Divine Being, whatever remained of the Life Spirit from the divine inspiration during the first creation of the world...

<insert>one leaf of text missing</insert>

<column-break/>

...remained in all sorts of creatures, He tucked all that same Spirit into this first-named mass, and locked it so firmly at the bottom in the depths of the earth. He indicated and left it to wise men to raise it, finish it, use it, and perform similar miracles with it through His holy wisdom, and He still arms and implants it daily.

The two aforementioned pieces—Sun and Moon, or Red and White, or rather the preparation of 🜍 <note>Alchemical symbol for Sulfur</note> and Mercury—are the ingredients in the composition of our Philosopher's Stone. For when the materials are first purified and cleansed through sufficient and oft-repeated <term>Sublimations</term> <note>The process of heating a substance until it turns to vapor and then becomes solid again.</note>, they are then diligently weighed and immediately composed therein. But you must not be ignorant of the power and opportunity of these two ingredients; rather, you must know how to set the weights of both according to the <term>Physical Proportion</term> <note>original: "proportionem Physicam"</note>. For a good portion of the Sulfur can be "blessed" (or united) with a small part of the <term>Soul of the Sun</term> <note>original: "animae Solis"</note> or Sulfur; then, by joining them with a graceful hand-movement, the preparation and the hardest work is accomplished.

But it must be known that you must first tinge your "Gum" with the Red <term>Tincture</term>. However, it does not become red <note>original: "in continenti"</note> immediately, but remains white. He—the Mercury—has the privilege that he wishes to be tinged first before all others, and the Philosophers report from where the "Soul of the Sun" for this Tincture of Mercury should be taken. The ferment of gold is gold, just as the ferment of dough is dough. Likewise, the ferment of gold is from its own nature, and its power is perfect only when it has been turned into an "Earth." For that is only the beginning for the Philosophers—the right, true <term>First Matter of the Metals of the Philosophers</term> <note>original: "Prima materia Philosophorum metallorum"</note>. From that point on, the masters who are truly experienced in the art first begin to stretch their ingenuity and arrive at the High Work. The artist then proceeds further with such work and, through God's blessing, brings it to the end for which it was intended and embodied by God—namely, to the highly blessed Philosopher's Stone. Thus, from nothing else but the <term>Secret Spirit of the Universe</term> alone is the true "First Matter" of the Philosophers arranged and prepared.

Now, he who rightly recognizes this Secret Spirit undoubtedly understands the secrets and wonders of Nature and possesses the knowledge of the <term>Light of Nature</term>. For it is a harmonic, sympathetic, and magnetic motion, from which arise the harmony and concord—the magnetic and sympathetic power or effect—of the Upper and Lower worlds. Note, however, that the natures of both ingredients are initially unlike one another because of their contrary qualities; for one is warm and dry, the other is cold and moist. These must by all means be united. When this is to happen, their contrary qualities must be gradually changed and reconciled. Take care that neither nature rises above the other through too strong a fire, for you would never be able to bring them together again. Both natures must rise together under the government of the fire; then the <term>Dyscrasia</term> <note>An ancient medical/alchemical term meaning an imbalance of elements or humors.</note> is removed from the Body, and an equality and good temperament is introduced, which happens through moderate and sustained cooking.

For when the two natures, Sulfur and Mercury, are enclosed in the narrow vessel and continued with moderate heat, they begin to let go of their contrary essence and agree with one another until they are finally qualified so that a "conspiracy" and simultaneous rising occurs. It stands at the top of the glass as <note>original: "numero eins"</note> number one; they are ready to be married. Then, say the Philosophers, the bridegroom places a golden ring on his bride. And when the Mercury and its Sulfur are cooked together like water and earth in the proper manner, they cast away all their superfluities, and the pure parts join together more and more as time passes, and are rid of their <term>Corlicibi</term> <note>Likely a corruption of a term for 'impurities' or 'dregs'.</note>; otherwise, the impure parts prevent the union and the "ingress" (penetration).

For the Mercury, as the first body, is quite coarse and cannot be mixed or perpetuated <note>original: "per minima"</note> in the smallest parts, for no body enters the other, nor is it truly and "at the root" united with it. But if matters are to be helped so that a true Tincture is prepared, a new spiritual body must be prepared from this, which has sprung from both. For after purification, one takes on the virtue of the other, and from many, one is made, <note>original: "numero & virtute"</note> in number and in power. However, if the fire were too strong and not governed according to the requirements of nature, these two aforementioned would either be suffocated or separated, since they would not have their lovely path, and the result would be either nothing or a ruined work...

It details the laboratory processes of solution, digestion, and the harmonic "marriage" of Sulfur and Mercury to create the Philosopher's Stone.</summary>

# THE EMERALD TABLET OF HERMES.

<image-desc>
An alchemical emblem consisting of a large circular diagram within a double-lined rectangular border. Around the inner border of the circle, the Latin motto reads: "VISIT THE INTERIOR OF THE EARTH; BY RECTIFYING [PURIFYING] YOU WILL FIND THE HIDDEN STONE" <note>This is the famous V.I.T.R.I.O.L. acronym, a mnemonic for the alchemical process: Visita Interiora Terrae Rectificando Invenies Occultum Lapidiem.</note>. Inside the circle, seven planetary symbols are displayed: at the top are the Sun (gold), the Moon (silver), and a golden chalice containing the symbol for Mercury. Below these are the symbols for Mars (red), Venus (green), Jupiter (tin/blue), and Saturn (lead/black). Two hands reach in from clouds on the left and right sides. Below the symbols, five shields and two globes are interconnected by a golden chain. The shields feature a green eagle, a red lion, and a central multicolored seven-pointed star topped with a globus cruciger. The two globes represent the celestial sphere (with stars) and the terrestrial sphere (with clouds and land).
</image-desc>

## THE WORDS OF THE SECRETS OF HERMES.

1. It is true, without falsehood, certain, and most true: that which is Below is like that which is Above, and that which is Above is like that which is Below, so that one may achieve and perform the miraculous wonders of a single thing. <note>This is the fundamental law of correspondence in alchemy: the microcosm (man/earthly matter) reflects the macrocosm (God/the heavens).</note> 
2. And just as all things were created from one thing alone, by the will and command of the One who conceived it: so also do all things now arise from this single thing through the order of Nature. 
3. Its father is the Sun, and its mother is the Moon; the Air carries it as if in its womb; its nurse or foster-mother is the Earth. <note>The Sun and Moon represent the masculine (sulfur) and feminine (mercury) principles, while Air and Earth represent the elemental mediums of the Great Work.</note> 
4. This thing is the origin of all perfections found in the world. 
5. Its power is most perfect when it is changed back into earth. <note>original: "wann es wieder in Erde verwandelt ist" — This refers to 'fixation,' making a volatile spirit solid and stable.</note> 
6. Then, separate the earth from the fire, and the subtle or thin from the thick or coarse, gently and with great understanding and discernment. 
7. It ascends from the earth to heaven, and from there descends again to the earth, and receives the power of the Above and the Below. 
8. Thus you will possess the glory of the entire world. 
9. Therefore, let all ignorance and incapacity depart from you. 
10. This is the strongest strength of all strengths: for it can overcome every subtle thing and can penetrate every hard or solid thing. 
11. Thus the world was created; from this, strange unions arise, and various wonders are worked, the way of which is this. 
12. For this reason, I have been called: <term>Hermes Trismegistus</term> <note>Hermes the "Thrice-Greatest," the mythical founder of alchemy. He is said to be "thrice-great" because he knows the three parts of the wisdom of the whole world: alchemy, astrology, and theurgy.</note>, as I possess three parts of the wisdom of the whole world. 
13. This is what is said concerning the masterpiece of the <term>chemical art</term> <note>original: "chymischen Kunst" — In this period, "chemistry" and "alchemy" were often used interchangeably to describe the art of transmuting matter.</note>.

Emerald Tablet, Hermes Trismegistus, Alchemy, Vitriol, Chemical Art

It outlines the philosophical principles of correspondence between the heavens and the earth and the process of creating the Philosopher's Stone.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the "single thing" mentioned in the Emerald Tablet, focusing on the practical and secretive nature of the alchemical "Subject" or raw material. It transitions from the theoretical foundation of the previous page to the specific behavior of the material during the Great Work.</meta>

...silence, which has placed a padlock on their own mouths and pressed a firm *seal* upon them; for if it were to become as common as brewing or baking, the world would surely perish.

This single *Matter* <note>original: *Res*; the thing or substance of the work</note> however, which *dissolves itself, coagulates itself, impregnates itself, kills itself, and brings itself back to life* <note>original Latin: *solvit se ipsum, coagulat se ipsum, se ipsum impraegnat, mortificat & vivicat*</note>, has had many seekers, but most have failed and lost their way in their investigations. For it is a thing that is closest to gold: and it is such a thing that the poor as well as the rich can obtain it, no matter where it may be. However, the *Philosophers* threaten *divine curse* <note>original: *execrationem divinam*</note> and call down the wrath of God upon anyone who *expressly* <note>original: *expresse*</note> names the *Subject* <note>The "Subjectum" is the raw, unnamed starting material for the Stone.</note> with their mouth.

The Almighty God also upholds this *curse* of the philosophers and answers their plea, as He has kept it under His hands for several thousand years now. This is how it is with the aforementioned *Subject*: for our <term>Magnesia</term> <note>In alchemy, Magnesia refers not to the modern mineral, but to the 'Prime Matter'—the chaotic, universal starting substance.</note> does not merely contain a small, proportionate *quantity* of the universal *Vital Spirit* <note>original: *Spiritus Vitalis*</note>, but is so well *condensed* and *compressed* by heavenly power that many have become "drunk" from its vapors where it lies in its place. It cannot help itself up, but when a wise man who is knowledgeable in such matters comes along—be it in the depths of a mountain or elsewhere he may encounter it—he takes a spoonful of that juice. For by the special and abundant grace of God, both the poor and the rich are free to reach for it. He then goes home to his house, behind the stove or to another room that pleases him, and begins to build and labor with it; for he can keep it so quietly that even his own household will not notice. For this natural work is not as messy as the "slop-work" of common *laborers*, such as coal-burning, smelting, refining, and the like; rather, it is a work that one can keep in a locked chest in whatever room he wishes, provided only that no cat gets to it. And if necessity requires it, he can carry out his trade alongside it, provided he knows how to set the furnace (which should be of triple-proven strength) with the right heat and let Nature take its course. <margin>*</margin> Now, when the *earthiness* <note>original: *Terrestritæt*</note> has been removed through *dissolution* <note>original: *Solution*</note>, sharpened through long *digestion*, freed from the *raw matter* <note>original: *crudæ materiæ*</note>, prepared into the most subtle form and reborn—and afterwards, this high-born, sharp, and powerful *Spirit* is added many times at certain intervals in a proper *quantity*, after the fashion of a drink or feeding, *by way of soaking* and *nourishing* <note>original: *per modum imbibitionis* and *nutritionis*</note>—its power is *condensed* over the aforementioned in such a way, and its daily "new pillars" arrive from its brothers and are driven into it. How then do you think such a work can be brought forth? For such power and immeasurably hidden strength of the *Vital Spirit* comes to the *raw matter* or *Subject* from the *stars* and *constellations* of heaven into its soil. From this, the Philosophers' *secret spirit of the universe* <note>original: *Spiritus universi secretus*</note> is drawn, which is the *Mercury* of the wise. It is the beginning, the middle, and the end, in which is enclosed and hidden the *Physical Gold* <note>original: *Aurum Physicum*</note>, which common *laborers* imagine they can extract from common gold, but in vain. Although the *Philosophers* treat much of *Sun* and *Moon* <note>Gold and Silver</note> in their writings, which are the most stable metals in <term>Fire</term> <note>original: the symbol Δ</note>, this is not to be understood literally. For their *Sun* and *Moon*, when brought to their inner *purity* and well-reconciled through the right, natural, proper, and philosophical *preparation*, are like the heavenly stars—the Sun and Moon—which illuminate day and night, the upper and lower *firmament*, with their clarity. Therefore, these two noble metals, the Philosophers' *Sun* and *Moon*, which are by nature similar to the human body, can pour in such high health to those who use them rightly and know how to prepare them. Beyond this and above it, nothing else is to be prepared except the single, threefold point of the *Universal*. It is the *Spirit* enclosed in these two that works such constancy, power, and virtue, as in other things.

Now, when a man graced by God can prepare and ready a thing from the aforementioned Red or White, or *Sun* and *Moon*—which is called the *Philosophers' Stone* <note>original: *Lapidem Philosophorum*</note> or the ancient "Water-stone of the Wise"—from a *creature* <note>In this context, 'creature' means any created thing in nature.</note> in which God placed such power at the creation of the world, or implanted it into the often-mentioned matter or *Subject* for the love and pleasure of highly gifted men of God... I hold that the Divine Being, whatever remained of the *Vital Spirit* from the *Inspiration* of the first *creation* of the world

<column-break/>

in all kinds of *creatures*, tucked all that same *Spirit* into this first-named *Mass* <note>original: *Massam*</note> and locked it firmly at the very bottom in the depths of the earth. He indicated and left it for wise men to raise up, finish, use, and perform similar *miracles* <note>original: *Miracula*</note> with it through His holy wisdom, and He still arms and implants it daily.

The two aforementioned pieces, such as Sun and Moon, or Red and White—or rather the *preparation* of <term>Jupiter (Tin)</term> <note>original: ♃is</note> and *Mercury*—these two pieces are the *ingredients* in the *composition* of our *Philosophers' Stone*. For when the *materials* are first purified and cleansed through sufficient and often-repeated *sublimations*, they are then diligently weighed and immediately composed therein. But you must not be ignorant of the power and occasion of these two *ingredients*; rather, you must know how to set the two *weights* according to *physical proportion* <note>original: *secundum proportionem Physicam*</note>. For a good part of <term>Jupiter (Tin)</term> can be blessed with a small part of the *soul of the Sun or Sulfur* <note>original: *animæ Solis vel Sulphuris*</note>; once you unite them with a graceful maneuver, the *preparation* and the hardest part of the work is accomplished.

But it is vital to know that you must first tincture your tin with the red *tincture*; however, it does not become red *immediately* <note>original: *in continenti*</note>, but remains white. *Mercury* has the privilege that, before all others, it wants to be tinctured first. The *Philosophers* report where this *Soul of the Sun* from this *tincture* of *Mercury* should be taken. The *ferment* of gold (is gold), just as the *ferment* of dough is dough. *Likewise*, the *ferment* of gold is from its own nature, and its power is perfect when it has been turned into an Earth. For that is only the Philosophers' beginning, the right, true *first matter of the philosophers' metals* <note>original: *prima materia Philosophorum metallorum*</note>. From that point on, the true masters, experienced in the Art, first begin to stretch their *ingenuity* <note>original: *Ingenium*</note> and arrive at the high work. The *Artist* <note>original: *Artifex*</note> then continues further with such work and, through God's blessing, brings it to the end toward which it is *inclined* and into which it was embodied by God—namely, to the highly blessed Stone of the Wise. Thus, from nothing else but the *secret spirit of the universe* alone is the true *first matter of the philosophers* readied and prepared. Whoever truly recognizes this *Secret Spirit* undoubtedly also understands the secrets and wonders of nature and possesses knowledge of the Light of Nature; for it is a *harmonic, sympathetic, and magnetic motion*, from which the *Harmony* and *Concordance*, the magnetic and sympathetic power or effect of the Upper and Lower, arise. But mark this: the natures of both *ingredients* are initially unlike each other because of their opposing qualities—for one is warm and dry, the other is cold and moist. These must now be united by all means. If this is to happen, their opposing qualities must be gradually changed and reconciled. Ensure that neither nature rises above the other through too strong a fire, for you would never be able to bring them together again. Both natures must rise together under the governance of the fire; then the *imbalance* <note>original: *Discrafia*; a medical/alchemical term for an imbalance of humors</note> is taken from the *body*, and an *equality* and good *temperament* are introduced, which occurs through a moderate and persistent boiling.

For when the two natures, *Sulfur and Mercury*, are enclosed in the narrow vessel and *continued* with moderate heat, they begin to let go of their opposing essences and harmonize until they are finally *qualified* so that a *unification* <note>original: *Conspiration*; literally "breathing together"</note> and simultaneous rising occurs. When it stands at the top of the glass as *number* one, they are ready to be married; then, the bridegroom puts a golden ring on his bride's finger, as the *Philosophers* say. And when *Mercury* and its *Sulfur* are boiled together like water and earth in the proper manner, they cast away all their superfluities, and the pure parts join together more and more. They are freed from their *husks* <note>original: *corticib:*</note>; otherwise, the impure parts prevent the union and the *entry* <note>original: *Ingress*</note>.

For *Mercury*, as the first *Body*, is quite coarse and cannot be mixed or perpetuated *in the smallest parts* <note>original: *per minima*</note>, because no *body* enters another, nor is united with it *truly* and *at the root* <note>original: *vere* and *in radice*</note>. But if things are to be helped so that a true *Tincture* is prepared, a new spiritual *body* must be prepared from this, which has sprung from both. For after *purification*, one takes on the virtue of the other, and from many, one is made, in *number and power* <note>original: *numero & virtute*</note>. However, if the fire were too strong and not governed according to the demands of nature, these two mentioned above would either be suffocated or torn apart, since they would not have their lovely progression, and the result would be either nothing or a ruined work.

<meta>This page presents the "Emerald Tablet," the foundational text of all Western alchemy. While the previous page discussed the physical properties and the secrecy surrounding the "Subject" or raw material, this page provides the philosophical and spiritual blueprint—the "Emerald Tablet of Hermes"—which explains the relationship between the macrocosm (the universe) and the microcosm (the individual/the work).</meta>

# THE EMERALD TABLET OF HERMES.

<image-desc>A circular alchemical emblem contained within a rectangular border. An outer green ring contains the Latin inscription: "VISIT THE INTERIOR OF THE EARTH; BY RECTIFYING YOU WILL FIND THE HIDDEN STONE" <note>original: "VISITA INTERIORA TERRAE RECTIFICANDO INVENIES OCCULTUM LAPIDEM"; the first letters of each word form the acronym V.I.T.R.I.O.L., a famous alchemical command to look deep within matter and the self</note>. Within the circle, a central vertical axis displays a golden chalice receiving liquid from a personified Sun and Moon; below the chalice is the symbol for Mercury, a golden ring, and a heraldic shield featuring a seven-pointed star topped with a globus cruciger <note>a cross-bearing orb representing Christ's dominion over the world</note>. Around this axis are alchemical symbols for the planets: Mars (red), Saturn (dark grey), Venus (green), and Jupiter (blue/grey). Two disembodied hands point toward the center from the sides. At the bottom, a green dragon or eagle stands on the left, and a red lion on the right. Between them are two globes: one depicting terrestrial elements/clouds and the other a celestial sphere with stars.</image-desc>

## THE WORDS OF THE SECRETS OF HERMES.

Truthful, without lies, certain, and most true: that which is Below is like that which is Above, and that which is Above is like that which is Below, so that one may obtain and perform the wonders of the one thing <note>This refers to the "Unity of All" (Unus Mundus), the idea that the physical and spiritual worlds are mirrors of one another</note>. And just as all things were created from one thing alone, by the will and command of the One who contemplated it: so also do all things now emerge from this single thing, through the order of Nature. Its father is the Sun, and its mother the Moon; the Air carries it as if in its womb; its nurse is the Earth. This thing is the origin of all perfection throughout the world. Its power is most perfect when it is turned back into earth. 

Separate then the earth from the fire, and the subtle or thin from the thick or coarse, gently and with great ingenuity and modesty. It rises from the earth toward heaven, and from there descends again to the earth, and receives the power of the Above and the Below. In this way, you shall have the glory of the whole world. Therefore, let all ignorance and inability depart from you. This is the strongest of all strength: for it can overcome all subtle things, and can penetrate every hard or solid thing. 

Thus was the world created; from this, strange unions arise, and many kinds of wonders are wrought, the way to work which is this. For this reason, I have been called: <term>Hermes Trismegistus</term> <note>meaning "Hermes the Thrice-Great," a legendary Hellenistic figure combining the Greek god Hermes and the Egyptian god Thoth</note>, for I possess three parts of the wisdom of the whole world. This is what is said concerning the masterpiece of the chemical art <note>original: "chymischen Kunst"; here meaning Alchemy, the "Great Work" of transforming both metal and the soul</note>.

<summary>This page contains the "Tabula Smaragdina" (Emerald Tablet), the most famous text in alchemical literature, which outlines the principle of "as above, so below." It provides the philosophical justification for the "Great Work," describing how a single substance moves through the elements to achieve perfection.</summary>

--- Page 117 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical and philosophical instructions following the "Emerald Tablet." While the previous page established the universal law of "as above, so below," this text focuses on the practical application: identifying the "Subject" (raw material) and the delicate process of uniting the "Sun" (Sulfur) and "Moon" (Mercury) through controlled heat.</meta>

...silence, which has placed a muzzle-lock on their mouths and pressed a firm seal upon them; for if it were to become as common as brewing and baking, the world would surely perish.

This single **Matter** <note>original Latin: "Res"</note>, however, which *dissolves itself, coagulates itself, impregnates itself, kills and brings itself back to life* <note>original Latin: "solvit se ipsum, coagulat se ipsum, se ipsum impraegnat, mortificat & vivificat"</note>, has had many seekers, but most have failed, losing their way in the search. For it is a thing that is closest to gold: and it is such a thing that the poor as well as the rich can obtain it, no matter where it may be. But the Philosophers threaten a *divine curse* <note>original Latin: "execrationem divinam"</note>, and call down the wrath of God upon anyone who speaks the name of the *Subject* <note>The "Subjectum" is the hidden raw material from which the Philosopher's Stone is made</note> expressly with his mouth.

Almighty God also upholds this *Curse* of the philosophers and answers their call, as it has been held in hands for several thousand years. This is how it stands with the aforementioned *Subject*: for our *Magnesia* <note>In alchemy, "Magnesia" refers to the "primitive matter," not the modern mineral magnesium</note> does not only contain a small, proportioned quantity of the universal *Vital Spirit* <note>original: "Spiritus Vitalis"</note>, but is so well condensed and made compact by heavenly power that many have become intoxicated by its vapor. It lies there in its place and can no longer help itself. If, then, an intelligent person who is knowledgeable in such materials comes upon it—be it in the depths of a mountain or wherever else he may find it—he takes a spoonful of that juice. For by the special and abundant grace of God, both poor and rich are free to reach for it. He then goes homeward to his house, behind the stove or to another room where it pleases him to be comfortable, and begins to build and labor with it. He can keep it so secretly that even his own household servants do not notice it. For this natural work does not proceed in such a messy way as the "slop-work" of common laborers—such as charcoal burning, smelting, refining, and the like—but is a work that one can keep in a locked chest in whatever room he wishes, provided only that no cat gets at it. And if necessity requires, he can carry on his trade quite well alongside it, as long as he knows how to set the furnace (which is made of triple-insulation) to the right heat and lets Nature take its course. 

When the *Earthiness* <note>original: "Terrestreitaet"</note> has been removed from it through *Solution*, and it has been activated through long *Digestion*, stripped of the *crude matter* <note>original: "crudae materiae"</note>, prepared to the subtlest degree and reborn, and then afterward the high-born—namely this sharp and powerful *Spirit*—is many times added at certain times in a proper quantity, after the fashion of a drinking-in and nourishment, *by way of soaking* and *nutrition* <note>original Latin: "per modum imbibitionis und nutritionis"</note>, and its power is condensed over the aforementioned in such a manner; and when daily new pillars from its brothers arrive and are driven into it—how do you think such a work can be brought forth? For such power and immeasurably hidden strength of the *Vital Spirit* comes to the *crude matter* or *Subject* from the stars and constellations of the heavens into its earth, from which the Philosophers' *secret Spirit of the universe* is then drawn. This is the *Mercury* of the Wise, and it is the beginning, the middle, and the end, in which is enclosed and hidden the *Physical Gold* <note>original: "Aurum Physicum"</note>, which common laborers imagine they can extract from common gold, but in vain. 

Although the Philosophers treat much of the Sun and Moon <note>original: "Sol und Luna," representing Gold and Silver</note> in their writings—which are the most stable among metals in the **Fire** <note>represented by the symbol $\triangle$</note>—this is not to be understood literally. For their Sun and Moon, when brought to their internal purity and well-matched through the right, natural, proper, and philosophical preparation, are like the heavenly stars, the Sun and Moon, which illuminate day and night, the upper and lower firmament, with their clarity. Therefore, these two noble metals—the Sun and Moon of the Philosophers—which are by nature like the human body, can pour in such high health to whoever uses them rightly and knows how to prepare them. Beyond and above this, there is nothing else to prepare but the single, threefold point of the *Universal*. It is the *Spirit* enclosed within these two mentioned things that is creative and works such stability, power, and virtue, as in other things.

Since the man graced by God can now prepare and ready a thing from the aforementioned Red or White, or Sun and Moon—which is called the *Philosopher's Stone* <note>original: "Lapidem Philosophorum"</note> or the ancient Water-Stone of the Wise—from a creature in which God placed such power during the creation of the world, or implanted into the often-mentioned Matter or *Subject* for the love and pleasure of the highly gifted men of God. I hold, however, that the Divine Being—whatever remained of the *Vital Spirit* from the *Inspiration*...

<column-break/>

...of the same into all kinds of creatures, placed all that same *Spirit* into this first-named mass, and locked it so firmly at the bottom in the depths of the earth. He indicated and left it to wise men to raise, prepare, and use it, and to perform similar *Miracles* with it through His holy wisdom, and He still arms and implants it daily.

The two aforementioned pieces, such as Sun and Moon, or Red and White, or rather the preparation of the Sun <note>represented by the symbol $\odot$is</note> and *Mercury*, which both pieces are then the *Ingredients* in the *Composition* of our *Philosopher's Stone*. For when the *Materials* are first purified and cleansed through sufficient and often repeated *Sublimations*, they are then diligently weighed and immediately *composed* therein. But what the power and opportunity of the two mentioned *Ingredients* are must not be unknown to you; rather, you must know how to set the two *Weights* <note>original: "Pondera"</note> *according to physical proportion* <note>original Latin: "secundum proportionem Physicam"</note>. For a good part of the Sun <note>$\odot$is</note> allows itself to be blessed with a small part of the soul of the Sun or of Sulfur <note>original: "animae Solis vel Sulphuris"</note>; then, with a delicate maneuver, unite them, and then the preparation and the hardest work is accomplished.

But it is to be known that you must first of all tinge your mind with the red tincture; it will not, however, become red *immediately* <note>original Latin: "in continenti"</note>, but remains white. He, the *Mercury*, has the priority that he wants to be tinged first before all others, and the Philosophers report from where this same tincture of *Mercury* should be taken, along with the *soul of the sun* <note>original Latin: "anima solis"</note>. The ferment of gold (is gold), just as the ferment of dough is dough. Likewise, the *Ferment* of gold is from its own nature, and then its power is complete when it has been turned into an earth. For that is only the beginning of the Philosophers, the right true *First matter of the philosophers' metals* <note>original Latin: "Prima materia philoſophorum metallorum"</note>. From then on, the masters who are right and experienced in the art first begin to stretch their ingenuity and come to the high work. The *Artificer* then proceeds further with such work and, through God's blessing, brings it to the end toward which it is *inclined* and into which it was embodied by God—namely, to the highly blessed Stone of the Wise—so that nothing else but the *Secret Spirit of the Universe* alone prepares and readies the true *First Matter of the Philosophers*. Whoever now truly recognizes this *Secret Spirit* understands without doubt the secrets and wonders of Nature and has the knowledge of the Light of Nature; for it is a *harmonic, sympathetic, and magnetic motion* <note>original Latin: "motus harmonicus Sympaticus und magneticus"</note>, from which the *Harmony and Concordance* <note>original Latin: "Harmonia und Concordantia"</note>, the magnetic and sympathetic power or effect of the Upper and Lower, arise.

Note, however, that the natures of both ingredients are initially unequal to each other because of their contrary qualities; for one is hot and dry, the other is cold and moist. These must now by all means be united. But if this is to happen, their contrary qualities must gradually be changed and balanced, and [one must ensure] that neither nature rises above the other through too strong a fire, for then you would never be able to bring them together. For both natures must rise together under the governance of the fire; then the *Imbalance* <note>original Latin: "Discrastia"; referring to a disharmony of qualities</note> is taken from the *Body*, and an *Equality* and good temperament is introduced, which happens through a moderate and continuous boiling.

For when the two natures, *Sulfur* and *Mercury*, are thus enclosed in the narrow space and continued with moderate heat, they begin to cease from their contrary being and agree with each other until they are finally qualified and, through a *Conspiration* <note>A "breathing together" or union</note> and simultaneous rising, they become and stay at the top of the glass as *number one* <note>original: "numero eins," meaning unity</note>. They are ready to be married; then the bridegroom puts a golden ring on his bride, as the Philosophers say. And when *Mercury* is thus cooked with its *Sulfur* like water and earth together in the proper way, they cast away all their superfluities, and the pure parts join together more and more over time, and they are rid of their <unclear>corlicibi</unclear> <note>likely a corruption of "corruptible parts" or "faeces" (dregs)</note>; otherwise, the impure parts prevent the union and the *Ingress* <note>The penetration of the tincture into the metal</note>.

For *Mercury*, as the first *Body*, is quite coarse and cannot be mixed *by the smallest parts* <note>original Latin: "per minima"</note> nor *permanently joined* <note>original: "perpetuiret"</note>, for no *Body* enters into another, nor is truly and *at the root* <note>original Latin: "in radice"</note> united with it. But if matters are to be helped so that a true Tincture is prepared, then a new spiritual *Body* must be prepared from this, which has sprung from both. For after the *Purification*, one takes on the virtue of the other, and from many, one is made, *in number and power* <note>original Latin: "numero & virtute"</note>. If, however, the fire were too strong and not governed according to the requirement of Nature, these two mentioned above would either be suffocated or severed, since they would not have their lovely course, and the result would be either nothing or a ruined work.

<insert>one leaf of text missing</insert>
