<meta>This page follows the discussion of the "Subject" or raw material of alchemy. While the previous page warned of the secrecy and divine curse surrounding the naming of this material, this page presents the foundational text of the entire Hermetic tradition: the Emerald Tablet. It provides the philosophical framework—the unity of the macrocosm and microcosm—that justifies the search for the "Subject" described previously.</meta>

# THE EMERALD TABLET OF HERMES.

<image-desc>A circular alchemical diagram within a rectangular frame. The border of the circle contains the Latin text: "VISITA INTERIORA TERRA RECTIFICANDO INVENIES OCCULTUM LAPIDEM" <note>original Latin: "Visit the interior of the earth; by rectifying [purifying] you will find the hidden stone." The initials of this sentence spell VITRIOL, a key alchemical substance and a metaphor for inner transformation.</note>. Inside the circle, seven planetary/metallic symbols are arranged: the Sun (Sol/Gold) and Moon (Luna/Silver) at the top, Mars (Iron) and Venus (Copper) below them, then Saturn (Lead) and Jupiter (Tin). In the center, a golden chalice sits above a Mercury (☿) symbol, which is connected by chains to three heraldic shields at the bottom. The left shield depicts a yellow bird (eagle or phoenix), the right shield depicts a red lion, and the center shield shows a seven-pointed star. Two hands reach out from clouds on the left and right sides of the circle, pointing towards the central symbols.</image-desc>

# THE WORDS OF THE SECRETS OF HERMES.

> Truly, without lies, certain, and most true: that which is Below is like that which is Above, and that which is Above is like that which is Below, so that one may achieve and perform the wonders of a single thing. And just as all things were created from one thing alone, by the will and command of the One who meditated upon it: so also do all things now arise from this one single thing, through the order of nature. Its father is the Sun, and its mother the Moon; the Air carries it as if in its womb; its nurse or foster mother is the Earth. This thing is the origin of all perfections found in the world. Its power is most perfect when it is changed back into earth. Separate then the earth from the fire, and the subtle <note>The "subtle" refers to the refined, spiritual, or gaseous state of matter.</note> or thin from the thick or coarse, gently and with great understanding and modesty. It rises from the earth toward heaven, and from there descends again to the earth, and receives the power of the things above and the things below. Thus you shall have the glory of the whole world. Therefore, let all ignorance and inability depart from you. This is the strongest strength of all strength: for it can overcome every subtle thing and can penetrate every hard or solid thing. Thus was the world created; from this arise marvelous unions, and various wonders are performed, the way to work which is this. Therefore I have been called: Hermes Trismegistus <note>original: "Hermes the Thrice-Greatest," a legendary Hellenistic figure combining the Greek god Hermes and the Egyptian god Thoth.</note>, for I possess three parts of the wisdom of the whole world. This is what is said of the masterpiece of the chemical art. <note>original: "chymischen Kunst," referring to Alchemy.</note>

<vocab>Emerald Tablet, Hermes Trismegistus, V.I.T.R.I.O.L., Alchemy, Chemical Art</vocab>

<summary>This page presents the "Tabula Smaragdina" (Emerald Tablet), the foundational cryptic text of alchemy that establishes the principle of "as above, so below." It serves as a philosophical map for the creation of the Philosopher's Stone through the union of celestial and terrestrial forces.</summary>
<keywords>Emerald Tablet, Hermes Trismegistus, macrocosm and microcosm, VITRIOL, alchemy, four elements, Sun and Moon, chemical art</keywords>

--- Page 120 ---
<meta>This page continues the alchemical exposition, moving from general theory to a specific emblematic interpretation of the Emerald Tablet, a foundational text of Hermeticism.</meta>

# Interpretation and Explanation of the Emerald Tablet of Hermes.

This image looks simple and slight,
But holds within it great and weighty things.
Indeed, it contains such a secret
Which is the highest treasure in the world.
For what has ever been heard of
That is higher on this earth?
Than to be at all times a master
To whom money never runs out.
And to have a healthy body besides,
Refreshing his life for so long
Until the predestined time,
Which no creature can exceed.
All this, as I have now stated,
The figure clearly holds within it.
One possesses the shield besides three others,
In which the Eagle, Lion, and Star are set,
An **Imperial Orb** <note>original: "Reichsapfel", a symbol of worldly and spiritual authority</note> also stands herein
Finely painted right in the middle,
Heaven and Earth in equal manner,
Are also placed herein with diligence.
The hands that reach toward one another,
Above them you see the symbols of the metals.
In the circle that surrounds this image,
One finds seven words written.
Therefore, I will now report
What each one signifies particularly,
Then show without fear
How it is called by name.
Wherein lies the secret thing of the Sages
In which one finds great power.
And how it is then prepared,
Everything follows one after the other.
The three shields signify in a sum,
**Salt**, **Sulfur**, and **Mercury**. <note>The "Tria Prima" or Three Primes, the foundational elements of all matter in alchemical theory.</note>
The Salt is a firm body,
The very last thing in the Art.
The Sulfur is the soul from then on,
Without which the body can do nothing.
Mercury is the spirit of power,
Holding both soul and body together.
Therefore he is called a mediator;
What is made without him has no stability.
For soul and body could not die,
Unless the spirit were also there.
So soul and spirit would exist no more,
If the body were not with them.
Also, body or spirit have no power,
Where the soul does not keep them company.
All this is understood of the Art:
The body takes shape and gives stability.
The soul colors and tinctures,
The spirit makes it fluid and penetrates.
Therefore, in this Art, there cannot be
One alone among the three.
The greatest secret also cannot stand;
It must have soul, spirit, and body.
But what the fourth may be,
From which the three spring freely,
The name of that same thing teaches you,
In the lower shield: the **seven-fold star**.
The Lion of the same color and power,
Also its nature and property,
Is shown by the Eagle, yellow and white;
Understand me well, for it requires diligence:

<column-break/>

The Imperial Orb demonstrates
The sign of this highest good.
Heaven and Earth, the four elements,
**Fire**, **Air**, **Water** are within.
The right foundation and true report
The two hands witness with an oath.
From which root-source come forth
All metals and everything else.
Now nothing remains but the seven words;
Hear further what they signify.
If you now understand this well,
You shall nevermore fail.
Each word signifies a city,
Each of which has only one gate.
☉ The first signifies **Gold**, it is diligently yellow.
☽ The second **Silver**, it is beautiful white.
☿ The third **Mercury** <note>The metal quicksilver</note> is likewise gray.
♃ The fourth is **Tin**, it is sky blue.
♂ The fifth signifies **Iron**, it is blood red.
♀ The sixth **Copper**, it is green without mockery.
♄ The seventh **Lead**, it is black as coal.
Mark what I mean, understand me well:
In the gates of these cities, truly,
Stands the foundation of the Art entirely.
For no city works anything alone;
The others must also be present.
Also, one could not go into any city
If the gates were kept shut.
And if they had no gates at all,
They would accomplish nothing whatsoever.
When these gates are together,
They bring forth a shine of seven colors.
They shine very brightly with one another,
Their power is beyond compare.
Its wonder you find not on earth,
Therefore hear further such a report.
Seven letters, seven words,
Also seven cities, and seven gates,
Seven times, also seven metals,
Also seven days, and seven numbers.
To this I mean seven herbs,
Also seven arts, and seven stones.
Therein stands the stability of all Art;
Well to him who ever found such.
If it is too difficult for you to understand,
Hear me further for a greater report.
Truly I manifest this to you
Quite bright and clear, without envy or hate,
How it is named with one word,
**Vitriol**, to him who knows it rightly. <note>An acronym for "Visita Interiora Terrae Rectificando Invenies Occultum Lapidem"—Visit the interior of the earth; by rectification you will find the hidden stone.</note>
If you calculate this in a **Kabbalistic manner** <note>original: "Cabalischer Weiß", referring to gematria or letter-number mysticism</note>
Often with all diligence,
**Fifty-seven** in number,
You will find calculated everywhere.
Do not let the work weary you;
Understand me rightly, so you will enjoy it.
For abundance, mark me even better,
It is a **water that does not wet**. <note>A classic alchemical paradox referring to the "Philosophical Mercury" or the universal solvent.</note>
From which the metals are born,
It becomes like ice, frozen so hard.
A wet dust, full of wind,
In which all virtues are within.
If you do not recognize such,
I may not call it anything else to you.

<summary>This page provides a verse-by-verse symbolic interpretation of an alchemical illustration of the Emerald Tablet, explaining the relationship between the Three Primes (Salt, Sulfur, Mercury) and the seven planetary metals. It concludes by revealing the secret substance "Vitriol" and the paradoxical nature of the alchemist's prime matter.</summary>
<keywords>Emerald Tablet, Hermes Trismegistus, Tria Prima, Vitriol, Alchemical Symbolism, Seven Metals, Kabbalah, Philosophical Mercury</keywords>

--- Page 121 ---
<meta>This page transitions from the symbolic imagery of the Emerald Tablet to a practical, yet still allegorical, description of the alchemical process. It outlines the various "paths" or methods used to achieve the Great Work, emphasizing purification and the correct management of heat.</meta>

Now I will give you notice,
How it should be prepared.
There are seven ways to the Art;
If you know none of them, you labor in vain.
First of all, you must know
**Purification** <note>original: "Reinigung"</note>, if you are to succeed.
And although this is twofold,
Only one is truly lacking.
The first work happens through it freely,
Without any other addition.
To this, without any distilling,
Only through its **putrefaction** <note>original: "putrificiern", the process of breaking down matter through decay to reach its essence</note>,
From all of its earthiness;
Then it is all prepared.

This first path has two roads;
Blessed is he who walks them rightly.
The first stretches through the strength of fire,
With and in itself—mark this well.
The other stretches further on,
Until one reaches the treasure and gain.
This happens through **dissolving** <note>original: "Soluiren"</note>,
And watering <note>original: "tränken", also known as imbibing</note> it again;
This must be undertaken,
So you come to the end of the fine arts.
After complete and perfect purification,
It is prepared and cooked by the **Sun**,
Or in **warm manure** <note>original: "warmem Mist"; alchemists used the steady, gentle heat of decomposing horse dung as a natural incubator</note> for its time,
Which stretches out quite long.
Until it becomes fixed and perfect,
Wherein the Jewel of the Sages is hidden.

The other paths are very subtle,
In which a great many fail.
For therein is the diligence of **distilling**,
And likewise of **sublimating**.
Also the separation of the **four elements**,
As they are named by the Sages.
Air, Water, Fire rectified;
The **Earth** <note>original: "Erd"</note> at the bottom has misled many.
It is regarded as a poor thing,
Yet all the power lies within it.
Some do not know how to separate it
From its **husks** <note>original: "Corticibus", the dross or outer coverings</note>, so both fail.
It is thrown behind the door,
But the Sage brings it forth,
Cleanses it snow-white and clear;
This is the foundation, I tell you truly.

But if you wish to separate it,
Then note that it is no simple matter.
For if it is not prepared,
You will err, I say by my oath.
Therefore you must have **Vinegar** <note>original: "Essig", referring here to a philosophical solvent rather than common kitchen vinegar</note>,
Which is known to the Sages;
Through it you will achieve the separation,
So that it no longer looks upon anything earthly,
Until Body and Soul must be parted—
Otherwise called Fire and Earth.
And once they are purified,

---

Then the **mixture** <note>original: "Mischung"</note> follows, mark this,
So it comes to a wonderful strength.
The **fixed** <note>original: "Fix"</note> is fixed with the **unfixed** <note>original: "Unfix", the volatile or spirituous</note>,
Provided the fire is governed rightly.
And it becomes completely perfect,
Much sooner than in a year.

<column-break/>

Now you have the whole path at length,
Wherein there are no more than two narrow ways.
In which one quickly fails and goes astray;
Otherwise, it all stands clear and pure.
The **Water of the Sages** <note>original: "Weisen Wasser"</note> is the one,
Which is **Mercury** <note>original: "Mercurius"</note> alone.
The other is called a **Vinegar** <note>original: "Essig"</note>;
It is recognized by few.
From the **Philosophical Iron**,
This Vinegar previously circles;
This is **Lord Aes** <note>original: "Herr Aes", referring to copper or the metallic base of the work</note> who delights them,
That is why they have concealed it so hard.
Many hundreds of forms and names are given,
According to what each has chosen for himself.
One path from the right, clear fountain;
Some have worked a whole year.
But many, through their art and cunning,
Have shortened such a long period.
In the speed of the free preparation,
As shown to them by **Alchemy**.
The preparation alone
Makes this **Stone** <note>The Philosopher's Stone</note> great and magnificent;
Although there is only one matter,
Which lacks nothing else at all
Except that it be **clarified** <note>original: "clarificiert"</note>;
Its name, however, has misled many.
Yet I have revealed enough to you,
In many ways, forms, and manners.
Therefore the names are many; I say,
Do not let yourself be led away from the path.

The ancients write in their script,
That it is a **Dragon** <note>original: "Track"</note>, a great poison.
Another calls it a **Serpent**, a monster;
It is also not expensive anywhere.
All people have it at once,
In all the world, both poor and rich.
It is the property of the metals,
Through which they triumphantly overcome.
A perfection of the same,
And place a golden crown upon it.
Now the **Practice** <note>original: "Practick"</note> is finished,
For him who understands it and knows the matter.
Only two things are left out,
Which you will find soon enough,
If you follow the right path
And care diligently for your work:
The **Composition** <note>original: "Zsammensetzung"</note> is the one,
Which the Sages have kept secret:
The nature of the fire also has secret cunning,
Therefore its ordering is the other:
So that one does not do too much to it,
Otherwise the whole game is lost.
One cannot be too subtle with it,
Just as the hen hatches the chick <note>A famous alchemical metaphor for slow, gentle, and constant heat</note>.
It should be so in the beginning;
Time itself gives the evidence:
For as the fire is turned,
Accordingly this treasure is born.
Be diligent, steadfast, peaceful, and pious;
Pray that God also comes to your aid:
If you attain this, then let it be
That the poor are always recommended to your care.

<summary>This page details the various "ways" of alchemical preparation, focusing on the roles of purification, distillation, and the separation of elements. It concludes with practical advice on fire management—likening it to a hen hatching an egg—and a moral exhortation to be pious and charitable.</summary>
<keywords>purification, putrefaction, four elements, Mercury, Philosophical Vinegar, Lord Aes, fire management, charity, Dragon, Philosopher's Stone</keywords>

--- Page 124 ---
<meta>This page serves as an introduction to Hermetic Philosophy, focusing on the nature and origin of the "First Matter" (Prima Materia). It uses allegorical language and alchemical symbolism to describe a universal substance that sustains life, often identified with the "Fountain of Miracles."</meta>

# Hermetic Philosophy.

<image-desc>A detailed alchemical engraving with hand-colored elements. At the top, a hand emerges from a dark cloud holding a cord with a weight or magnet above a sunflower. A winged cherub's head in a smaller cloud blows a gust of wind toward the flower. Below the flower are three black birds standing on a horizontal line. Centrally, a large inverted triangle is inscribed with "Fountain of Miracles." Inside the triangle, a red rose and white lilies grow. Flanking the top corners of the triangle are the Sun and the Moon with human faces. At the bottom tip of the triangle, liquid flows into a circular vessel labeled "Juice of the Moon & Sun." From this vessel, two clouds of vapor rise, containing various alchemical symbols (including Mercury, Sulfur, Salt, and various metals). The entire scene is framed by a double-line border.</image-desc>

I draw all those who seek God and the truth; they alone will find the Art <note>The "Art" refers to Alchemy, the sacred process of transformation.</note>. I am the lodestone of divine love; I draw iron-hearted men onto the path of truth.

->First Matter.<-
<note>original: "Prima Materia"</note>

> Bloody, animal Rose of Jericho,
> Spiritual. Bright, silvery,
> milky-distilled from white lilies
> in the Valley of Jehoshaphat.
<note>original: "Sanguinalis animalis Rosa Hierichuntis / Spiritualis. Lucida, argentea, / lactea-stillata ex candida Lilia / in Valle Josophat."</note>

->FOUNTAIN of Miracles.<-
<note>original: "FONS Miraculorum"</note>

I am the sap that sustains all in nature and makes it alive, and I come from the upper into the lower; I am the dew of heaven and the fatness of the earth; I am the fiery water and watery fire, without me nothing may live in time; I am near to all things, yes, in and through all things, yet unknown.
Yet I alone am the hand of the philosophers.
I unlock and close again,
This brings the artist good peace,
Without me you will be able to do nothing
That serves your purposes;
Therefore fear God, pray and work in patience,
If you find me, you will emerge from need, and have a merciful God who is kind to you, and gives what your heart desires.

<margin>Juice of the Moon & Sun.</margin>
<margin>This juice must be caught before it turns into vapor or smoke.</margin>
<margin>The two vapors or smokes are the roots of the Art.</margin>

The <term>First Matter</term> <note>Prima Materia: the primitive, formless base of all matter sought by alchemists.</note> has its sustenance from the *Fiat* <note>The divine "let it be" from the creation story in Genesis.</note> and Word of Creation. And the Word is from the Father, through which all things are made, and the Spirit proceeds from both, and is the divine life-giving air. Thus the air in the elements makes all things alive. The fire warms all things, the water refreshes, restores, and drenches all things; and the nitrous earth, as a mother, nourishes and sustains all things; thus the fire has given birth to the air, and the air blows upon the fire in turn so that it lives; but the air, changed into water, is the fire's food, and in this element—water and fundamental moisture—as in the greasy, fatty fundamental moisture, the fire burns, and the earth, as a holder of <term>saltpeter</term> <note>Nitersalz: Alchemists believed saltpeter contained a celestial, life-giving spirit from the air.</note>, provides the nourishment for it, and in her womb dwell all these elements, for in this womb is the sulphuric saltpeter of nature, the one good thing that God has created in this visible world.

This same Salt-Mother of the elements is the nitrous, aluminous, spiritual, gummy water, <term>⊝</term> <note>The symbol for Salt.</note> earth or crystal, which nature has in her womb—a son of the Sun, and a daughter of the Moon. It is a <term>Hermaphrodite</term> <note>An alchemical symbol for the union of opposites, such as male/female or Sun/Moon.</note>, which the wind has carried in its belly; a <term>Phoenix</term>, living in fire; a <term>Pelican</term> <note>A symbol of sacrifice and revival, believed to feed its young with its own blood.</note>, who brings its dead young back to life with its blood; the young Icarus drowned in the water, whose nursing mother is the earth, the wind its mother, the fire its father, the water its cleanser and drink, a stone and no stone, a water and no water, and yet a stone of living power, and a water of living might; a <term>Sulphur</term>, a <term>Mercury</term>, a Salt, which nature carries hidden within her, and which no foolish person has ever known or seen.

---

->God sells His gifts for labor.<-
<note>original: "Deus vendit sua dona pro labore"</note>

<summary>This page describes the "First Matter" (Prima Materia) through rich alchemical metaphors, presenting it as a universal, life-giving substance born from the elements and divine Word. It emphasizes that this secret substance, while hidden in nature, is the essential foundation for the alchemist's work.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the mystical exploration of the human heart, likely from a 17th or 18th-century Pietist or Theosophical tradition (such as that of Jakob Böhme). It focuses on the internal transition from the "Old Creature" (sinful nature) to the "New Creature" (spiritual rebirth).</meta>

# Representation of the Human Heart of the Old and New Creature.

->G O D<-

<margin>This figure explains the First Epistle of John, and the same explains this figure.</margin>

<margin>The starry sky in the heart with its effect and powers is subject to vanity, and once time has passed, then everything returns to eternity.</margin>

<margin>A heart is narrow below, and very wide above, so that it may be open to God, but barred from worldliness.</margin>

**This writing must be understood from the inside out and** Everything that is in the great world <note>the Macrocosm or the universe</note> is also in man, for he is created from it; therefore he is the little world <note>the Microcosm</note>, and has everything in the center point of his heart. Mark this well.

**from the outside in.** God has reborn all people out of love, and has already relit the light in them while still in the mother’s womb; He himself is the light, the morning star, shining from the inside out.

<image-desc>A large heart-shaped diagram containing concentric circles and a central sun-like emblem. At the very top, inside a radiant sun, is the text "CHRIST at the Right Hand of God". The heart contains five concentric circles labeled No. 1 to No. 5. No. 1 (outermost) features stars and planetary symbols. No. 2 is labeled "Earth". No. 3 is labeled "the WORD". No. 4 is labeled "the NEW SPIRIT". No. 5 (center) is a golden sun with a six-pointed star and various letters within it. Flanking the bottom of the heart are two triangles representing the Trinity. The left triangle contains "GOD", "WORD", and "SPIRIT" with "Word all in all" in the center. The right triangle contains "FATHER", "SON", and "HOLY SPIRIT" with "God all in all" in the center.</image-desc>

God is threefold, the Word is threefold, and 2 times 3 is 6, concerning the inner person and the morning star in our hearts, which is JESUS CHRIST, the single point.

Jesus Christ yesterday, today, and in eternity; He who is, who was, and who is to come; the beginning and the end, Alpha and Omega <note>original: "A und O"</note>; in him the fullness of the Godhead has become bodily. **Hallelujah, Glory to God in the highest <note>original Latin: "Gloria in Excelsus Deo"</note>. Amen.**

->The old birth of death in the darkness must be<-
->killed through the cross and suffering; reason in the wide circle is captured, and man is<-
->led back through the Word into the Spirit, into the rebirth, into the light,<-
->in Christ; there alone is rest, peace, eternal life and the kingdom of heaven.<-

**No. 1.** Here, **CHRIST** is born as a mortal man from Mary according to the flesh; of this he says in John 6, "the flesh is of no use." Here is man’s birth from Adam, the old creature, sinful, mortal, which does not enter the kingdom of heaven. Man lives in darkness, blindness, night, and death, in his own reason derived from the power of the stars and the four elements; from these arise diseases, and the invention of trades, arts, high schools <note>universities</note>, and religious and worldly offices and estates; such things are in the heart. Over this rules the authority ordained by God. All of this is vanity and belongs to death; into this darkness the light shines, and the darkness cannot comprehend it. To this belong Christians, Jews, Turks <note>Muslims</note>, and Heathens; they are all together sinners and lack the glory they should have in God; they are all enclosed under the wrath of God.

**No. 2.** Here, **CHRIST** is troubled in spirit, tempted by the devil; the internal conscience derived from the stars is tempted by the lust of the eyes, the lust of the flesh, and a prideful life, with false security and despair. Here, the Holy Scripture is the dead letter <note>literalism without spiritual understanding</note>; it creates sects, superstition, and fleshly priests who fear that God's Word will be taken from them, yet they only mean the dead letter. The authorities want to defend religion with the sword and kill the true believers, Christ, and his apostles, thinking they do God a service thereby. Divine things are pure foolishness to them; they cannot understand Christ except according to the flesh and the lineage of David; they create divisions, factions, and sects.

**No. 3.** Here is the separation of light from darkness; the dawn rises, all temporal things are abandoned. Through much tribulation we must enter the kingdom of God; the person becomes a fool to the world. Christ is crucified in him, and he in Christ; therefore he is a cross to the world, and the world is a cross to him. Here stands yieldedness <note>original: "Gelassenheit," a central mystical term for surrendering the self-will to God</note>; whoever turns toward the light lives in Christ, and Christ in him, in time and eternity.

**No. 4.** The old must pass away entirely; behold, I make all things new. Here is the rebirth from water and spirit, from above, through the word of truth; a new creature born of God. Christ becomes flesh within it, rises in the person, wakes him from the dead, and feeds him with his true flesh and blood unto life. Christ is the Word of the Father, the Book of Life, the Gospel, the power of God; he works faith and brings salvation. He is King, ruling with the sword of the spirit; the person becomes of a divine nature and has his conduct in heaven with Christ.

**No. 5.** The Holy Spirit is the anointing, teaching the person **Divine** things inwardly; the person is a temple of God, in which the Holy Trinity dwells. Christ in man, God and man, working love. **CHRIST** all in all, the only ONE.

->**O LORD, merciful GOD, open the hearts of men through your HOLY SPIRIT to understand your mystery.**<-

--- Page 132 ---
<meta>This page continues the mystical exploration of the Fall of Man, focusing on the symbolism of the Tree of Knowledge as a source of both division and potential restoration. It builds on previous themes of divine unity versus earthly multiplicity.</meta>

# The Tree of Knowledge of Good and Evil

<insert>the Tree of Knowledge Good and Evil</insert>

<image-desc>A large symbolic engraving of the "Tree of Knowledge of Good and Evil." The tree is anthropomorphized, with the trunk taking the form of a human torso and outstretched arms, reminiscent of a crucified figure. The branches grow from the shoulders and head, bearing round fruits. At the heart of the figure is a radiant triangle containing a single eye, surrounded by a circle with the words "UNI: VERSALE." Around this central eye are three blocks of letters: "STU," "DI," and "UM." The figure is set within a large radiant sun-like circle containing stars and astrological symbols (zodiac signs like Libra, Scorpio, Sagittarius). Above the figure's head is another smaller radiant sun with a face. Text columns flank the upper part of the image, and a large block of text occupies the bottom half of the page.</image-desc>

It is a single tree that bears two kinds of fruit. Its name is the Tree of Knowledge of Good and Evil. As its name is, so also are its fruits: namely, good and evil, fruits of life and of death, of love and of wrath, of light and of darkness. This tree was presented to Adam in his innocence; and although he had the freedom to gaze upon it as a tree of God’s wonders, divine prohibition would not permit him to set his desire upon it or eat from it. Instead, God threatened him, saying that he would die from its fruit of death. For it was the tree of **differentiation** <note>original: "Scheidlichkeit"; refers to the state of being divided or separated into opposites</note>, where good and evil struggled with one another in division. In such a struggle, however, life cannot persist, for conflict gives birth to fragility, and fragility gives birth to death. Life, however, dwells in the sweet unity of love. Therefore, when Adam ate from this tree, the conflict became active within him, and in this conflict, he was forced to lose his life.

<column-break/>

Nevertheless, miserable mankind will not yet become any wiser through such a fall and injury. His desire is always fixed upon this tree and its fruits. He lusts after the **differentiation of multiplicity** <note>the state of being many things rather than one</note> and is always in conflict, even though he could enter into rest if he would only turn back to the unity of simplicity. The light of life stands in the center to show man the way to the first rest, and the Father in heaven lets his sun rise over the evil and the good. But everything grows according to its own kind, and man is more inclined to look upon the stars of multiplicity, which he chooses as his guides according to his own whim. Yet these often lead him far away from the true light and keep him trapped in the whirlpool of uncertainty, which winds itself further and further out from the internal countenance of the sun into the exterior, finding no end or place of rest—unless he winds himself back inside from the outside and seeks the Beginning, from which all smaller starlight originated.

---

Thus, among seven stars, there is hardly one that turns its rays inward so that they might point the searching mind toward Bethlehem; and among seven eyes that wind themselves around in the whirlpool of searching desire, there is hardly a single one directed toward the Sabbath in the interior. Instead, the restless mobility of the **working days** <note>original: "Werfel-Tage"; literally "tossing days," referring to the six days of labor and worldly distraction</note> drives them through all spheres. And though they may catch a glimpse of the wonders of God, they see them only from the outside because they are turned toward the exterior. Each eye sees only that toward which it is led by its own desire.

Man was created by God for a perpetual Sabbath; he was not meant to work himself, but to let God work within him. He was not to take anything for himself with his own hands, but only to receive what was abundantly offered to him by God’s goodness. However, he left the Sabbath and wanted to work himself; he stretched out his hand against the commandment to take, in his own desire, what he was not permitted to take. Therefore, God let him fall, and because he had despised rest, he thereafter had to feel unrest with pain. In such unrest of a self-driven life, all children of men still constantly stretch out their hands, wanting to grasp that through which they seek to attain their pleasure; and as their understanding and will are, so too is their grasping. Some hands reach for the good; some hands reach for the evil. Some reach for the fruit; some only for the leaves; some grasp a branch with both fruit and leaves. Now, whatever each person has grasped, he delights in it, and these poor people do not know that all their effort and labor is only a <term>particular study</term> <note>original: "studium particulare"; a specialized or fragmented focus on one part of knowledge rather than the whole</note>. 

They all reach for piecework and cannot attain the whole. They seek rest and find it not, for they seek it from the outside in the unrest of motion, while it actually dwells in the inner stillness of the internal **center** <term>Centro</term>. And even if one person grasps more than another, it is still only piecework. Sometimes, among seven hands, there is one that comes near to the mystery and grasps the whole trunk of the tree at the place where the divided, opposing kingdoms of its branches return into unity. Yet even that hand is still far from the root of the tree; it grasps and feels the mystery only from the outside and does not yet see it rightly from within. 

For the root of this tree—which goes through the **sphere** <term>Sphæram</term> of the visible world (mixed of good and evil) into the spheres of the invisible world, where light and darkness dwell in themselves—is understood only by the Eye of Wisdom, which stands in the **center** <term>Centro</term> of all spheres. This eye gazes in the highest rest upon the wonders of all movements and sees through all other eyes which wander around outside of rest in unrest, wanting to see for themselves without the true Eye of Wisdom, even though they all received their sight from that very source. This eye can test all spirits to see how sensible, pure, and sharp they are. It understands where good and evil spring from. Light and darkness are manifest before it. Time and eternity, the visible and invisible, the present and future, the earthly and heavenly, the bodily and spiritual, the high and the deep, the external and internal—all are understood by it. And yet, it is troubled by none of these, for it dwells in the **center** of rest, where everything stands in equality beyond conflict. What it sees, it also possesses. For in the **center** of its rest is the royal throne to which all is subject.

Therefore, O dear man! If you wish to return to the right understanding and the right rest, then cease from your works and let God alone work in you. Then the Eye of Wisdom will open within you, and you will attain **from a particular study to the universal** <note>original: "a studio particulari ad universale"</note>, and find everything in the One.

<summary>This page uses the allegory of the Tree of Knowledge to explain how human desire for "multiplicity" and fragmented knowledge leads to unrest and death. It advocates for a return to "unity" and "inner rest" (the Sabbath), where the Eye of Wisdom allows one to see the whole of creation from a divine center.</summary>

<keywords>Tree of Knowledge, Good and Evil, Adam, Sabbath, Particular Study, Universal, Rest, Unity, Multiplicity, Eye of Wisdom, Center, Sphere, Spiritual Alchemy</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the English verse translation of "The Philosophers' Mountain," an alchemical allegory likely transcribed from a German Rosicrucian source. It completes the description of the symbolic journey and the chemical stages (Nigredo, Albedo, Rubedo) required to achieve the "Crystal" or Philosophers' Stone.</meta>

Groping around the wall they come
With rags of Sophistry <note>Sophistry: deceptive or fallacious reasoning, here referring to those who pursue alchemy through false logic or greed.</note> behung.
On his right hand, the Hare yonder <note>The Hare is a traditional symbol for the seeker's alertness and the "investigation" required in the work.</note>
Denotes the Art of Chemistry,
A strange & wonder working Art
Proved only by the fires fierce dart.
On his left hand you ~~stand~~ plainly see
What the true Key of Art may be.
Like as inflames the Hen her Mate
So none can this too subtil <note>subtle: here meaning refined, volatile, or difficult to grasp.</note> make.

Half up the Mount before the door,
Stands the bold <gloss>brave</gloss> Lion, in great honour; <note>The Lion often represents "Sulfur" or the fixed, masculine principle in alchemy.</note>
whilst the huge Dragon standing near,
Pours out his noble blood so dear; <note>The "blood of the dragon" is a common metaphor for a potent solvent or the vital energy released during a reaction.</note>
In deepest grave, now throw him in,
From which the Coal-black Raven springs; <note>The Raven represents "Nigredo," the stage of putrefaction, blackening, and the death of the old form.</note>
And which is named "The Gate of Art"
The Snow-white Eagle thence up starts. <note>The Eagle represents "Albedo," the stage of purification, whitening, and sublimation where the matter is "elevated."</note>
The Crystal fine in furnace bright
Itself reveals to thy Eye sight.

† Investigation, Search, and so forth. <note>original: "† Investigation, Search, &c." This note links the symbol of the Hare to the active pursuit of knowledge.</note>

**Key Vocabulary**

*   **Sophistry:** False or misleading arguments.
*   **Art of Chemistry:** The practice of alchemy.
*   **Key of Art:** The fundamental secret or starting material.
*   **Bold Lion:** The fixed metallic principle.
*   **Dragon:** The volatile principle or raw matter.
*   **Coal-black Raven:** The stage of decomposition (Nigredo).
*   **Gate of Art:** The beginning of the true transformation.
*   **Snow-white Eagle:** The stage of purification (Albedo).
*   **Crystal:** The final, perfected state of the matter.
*   **Furnace:** The alchemical oven (Athanor) used for constant heat.

<summary>This page concludes an alchemical poem describing the symbolic ascent of the "Philosophers' Mountain," detailing the transition from Nigredo (the Raven) to Albedo (the Eagle). It serves as a guide to the spiritual and physical transformations necessary to reveal the "Crystal" or Philosophers' Stone.</summary>
<keywords>Alchemy, Rosicrucianism, Sophistry, Nigredo, Albedo, Philosophers' Stone, Symbolism, Chemical Wedding</keywords>

->Copied from a written manuscript very similar to this one, though not as large, in the Freemason Hall, London.<-

->Frederick Holland, Rosicrucian, & Member of the Metropolitan College, 30° ancient & primitive, 90° memphis, 360° mizraim.<- <note>These titles refer to high ranks in specific Masonic and Rosicrucian rites popular in the 19th century.</note>

<summary>This page contains elaborate alchemical tables and correspondences based on the "Seven Properties of Nature" by the mystic Jacob Boehme. It maps planetary symbols, metals, and virtues onto theological concepts like the Trinity (Adonai) and the nature of the Soul.</summary>
